## R-236 · OFDM numerology: 802.11a/g, LTE and 5G NR cyclic prefixes

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: 3GPP specifications as published by ETSI (primary), an IEEE 802.11 working-group document (the published IEEE Std 802.11 now requires a sign-in and was not opened), cross-checked against ETSI HIPERLAN/2, Keysight and MathWorks documentation. Tagged [Established]; quotations as the subagent recorded them; values marked derived are arithmetic from the quoted ones.*

- **802.11a/g, 20 MHz** (IEEE 802.11 doc. 11-05/1102r4, Table 1, legacy column; ETSI TS 101 475 V1.3.1 Table 6 for the identical HIPERLAN/2 numerology; MathWorks `wlanNonHTOFDMInfo`): 64 subcarriers at 20 MHz, "312.5kHz (20MHz/64)"; 48 data and 4 pilot subcarriers (52 used); "TFFT: IFFT/FFT period 3.2µsec"; "TGI: Guard Interval length 0.8µsec= TFFT/4" (16 samples; `CPLength: 16`); "TSYM: Symbol Interval 4µsec". 802.11n: "TGIS: Short Guard Interval length 0.4µsec= TFFT/8", optional on transmit and receive. Derived: prefix overhead 16/80 = 20%. [11-05/1102r4](https://mentor.ieee.org/802.11/dcn/05/11-05-1102-04-000n-joint-proposal-phy-specification.doc); [ETSI TS 101 475](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/101400_101499/101475/01.03.01_60/ts_101475v010301p.pdf)
- **LTE** (ETSI TS 136 211 V19.3.0 = 3GPP TS 36.211 Rel-19): "Ts = 1/(15000 × 2048) seconds"; N = 2048 for Δf = 15 kHz; Table 6.12-1 normal cyclic prefix "160 for l = 0", "144 for l = 1,2,...,6"; extended 512; "Tslot = 15360 · Ts = 0.5 ms". Derived: 30.72 MHz sampling, 66.67 µs useful symbol, 5.208 and 4.6875 µs prefixes, prefix overhead (160 + 6 × 144)/15360 = 6.67%. [TS 136 211](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/136200_136299/136211/19.03.00_60/ts_136211v190300p.pdf)
- **5G NR** (ETSI TS 138 211 V19.5.0 = 3GPP TS 38.211 Rel-19): Tc = 1/(Δfmax·Nf), Δfmax = 480 kHz, Nf = 4096, κ = Ts/Tc = 64; Table 4.2-1: Δf = 2^μ·15 kHz for μ = 0..6, extended cyclic prefix only for μ = 2; clause 5.3.1: useful part 2048κ·2^−μ, normal prefix 144κ·2^−μ (plus 16κ for l = 0 or 7·2^μ), extended 512κ·2^−μ, in units of Tc. Derived: normal prefix 4.6875 µs at μ = 0, 0.293 µs at μ = 4 (240 kHz), 0.073 µs at μ = 6. [TS 138 211](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/138200_138299/138211/19.05.00_60/ts_138211v190500p.pdf)
- Not confirmed: the clause and table numbers of the timing table in the current IEEE Std 802.11; the claim that 240 kHz spacing is used only for synchronization blocks.

## R-237 · ADC dynamic testing: IEEE 1241, ENOB conventions, coherent sampling, a data-sheet example

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (curl through the build proxy): the IEEE SA standard pages, TI documents and data sheet, a Renesas application note and a Rohde & Schwarz technical paper. The text of IEEE 1241 itself was not opened (paywalled), so its ENOB definition is reported second-hand. analog.com refused automated access and was not used. Quotations as the subagent recorded them.*

- **IEEE Std 1241-2023** ("Standard for Terminology and Test Methods for Analog-to-Digital Converters") is the active edition: Board approval 2023-06-05, published 2023-10-06, superseding 1241-2010 (now "Inactive-Reserved"). No revision project was listed in Nov 2026. [Established] [IEEE SA 1241-2023](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/1241/6797/)
- **ENOB, SINAD form:** TI *Analog Engineer's Pocket Reference* (SLYW038D, rev. Apr 2025): "ENOB = (SINAD(dB) − 1.76 dB) / 6.02", with SINAD defined relative to "the RMS equivalent of the ADC's full-scale input" (full-scale-referred). [Established] [TI SLYW038D](https://www.ti.com/document-viewer/lit/html/SLYW038D/GUID-62961252-5D9A-4098-A630-21BD49A27DB2)
- **ENOB, IEEE 1241 form (second-hand):** Rohde & Schwarz technical paper 1ER03_1e (2011), citing IEEE 1241-2010: ENOB = 0.5·log2(SINAD) − 0.5·log2(1.5) − log2(A/V), with A the fitted sine's peak-to-peak amplitude and V the full-scale range; "any input amplitude can be used, since the difference between the full-scale amplitude and the actual test amplitude is taken into account". Derived (ours): this equals (SINAD_dBFS − 1.76)/6.02, the TI form. [R&S 1ER03_1e (PDF)](https://scdn.rohde-schwarz.com/ur/pws/dl_downloads/dl_application/application_notes/1er03/ENOB_Technical_Paper_1ER03_1e.pdf)
- **Coherent sampling:** TI SLVAG04 (R. Reeder, Feb 2025): "To get coherent sampling, you need to bin-center the input signal – contain all energy to a single bin"; choosing a prime bin number further improves the measurement; no window for coherent captures, Blackman-Harris for non-coherent ones. Renesas AN9675 (rev. 1.00, Oct 2026): f_IN/f_S = M/N; "M should be odd or prime... A prime M eliminates all common factors with N"; "Common factors between M and N lead to different harmonics of fIN having the same frequency bin". [Established] [TI SLVAG04](https://www.ti.com/lit/pdf/slvag04); [Renesas AN9675](https://www.renesas.com/en/document/apn/an9675-coherent-and-windowed-sampling-ad-converters)
- **Data-sheet example:** TI ADC12DJ5200RF (12-bit, 5.2 GSPS dual / 10.4 GSPS single; SLVSEN9H, rev. Aug 2026), dual-channel typicals at fIN = 347 MHz, AIN = −1 dBFS, fCLK = fS = 5.12 GHz: SNR 55.6 dBFS, SINAD 54.5 dBFS, ENOB 8.8 bits, SFDR 65 dBFS; at 2397 MHz: SNR 54.7, SINAD 53.9 dBFS, ENOB 8.7 (min 8.18), SFDR 65. SNR "excluding DC, HD2 to HD9, fS / 2, fS / 2 – fIN". The front page states "ENOB (dual channel, FIN = 2.4GHz): 8.6 Bits", 0.1 bit below the table's typical. FFT length and window are not stated. [Established] [ADC12DJ5200RF data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/adc12dj5200rf.pdf)
- Perishable: data-sheet revisions change typical values; re-read before citing numbers.
- **ADC12DJ5200RF, further figures** (added 20 Nov 2026; read by an independent review subagent from data sheet SLVSEN9H, revised August 2026, and by a research subagent for the noise density): SINAD at −12 dBFS input is 56.1 dBFS at 347, 997 and 2397 MHz; aperture jitter 50–60 fs rms; SINAD at −1 dBFS falls to 46.2 dBFS at 8 GHz; noise spectral density −152.0 dBFS/Hz in dual-channel mode with 1.0 Vpp full scale (−150.4 dBFS/Hz at the default 0.8 Vpp), with A_IN = −20 dBFS. [Established] [ADC12DJ5200RF data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/adc12dj5200rf.pdf)

- **ADC12DJ5200RF noise by mode (SLVSEN9H, revised Aug 2026, read 21 Nov 2026, primary):** dual-channel mode (§6.7): NSD −152.0 dBFS/Hz at VFS = 1.0 Vpp and −150.4 at 0.8 Vpp, noise figure (ZS = 100 Ω) 23.0 and 22.6 dB; single-channel mode: NSD −154.2 and −152.9 dBFS/Hz, noise figure 20.8 and 20.0 dB. [Established]

## R-238 · Converter digital filters: ADS127L11/ADS127L21 decimation filters, PCM5122 interpolation filters

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (curl through the build proxy, PDFs converted with pdftotext); the ADS127L11 wideband-filter rows and the PCM5122 filter rows were re-read by the main author from the converted text. Quotations as recorded.*

- **TI ADS127L11** ("400-kSPS, Wide-Bandwidth, 24-Bit, Delta-Sigma ADC"), data sheet SBAS946C, April 2021, revised September 2022. §6.5 Electrical Characteristics, "WIDEBAND FILTER CHARACTERISTICS" (TA = 25 °C, AVDD1 = 5 V, VREFP = 4.096 V, fCLK = 25.6 MHz, high-speed mode): pass band "Within envelope of pass-band ripple 0.4 ∙ fDATA", "–0.1-dB frequency 0.4125 ∙ fDATA", "–3-dB frequency 0.4374 ∙ fDATA"; pass-band ripple −0.0004 to 0.0004 dB; "Stop-band frequency … 0.5 · fDATA"; "Stop-band attenuation(1) 106 dB", footnote: out-of-band inputs "intermodulate with the chop frequency beginning at fMOD / 32, which results in stop-band attenuation exceeding 106 dB"; "Group delay 34 / fDATA"; "Settling time 68 / fDATA". §8.3.5.1: "multistage FIR filter design featuring linear phase response"; the group delay "is constant versus frequency". The low-latency filter is a sinc (CIC) filter (§8.3.5.2). [Established] [ADS127L11 data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/ads127l11.pdf)
- **TI ADS127L21**, data sheet SBASAK4C, March 2023, revised August 2025: default FIR filter specifications identical to the ADS127L11's; adds a "PROGRAMMABLE FIR3 FILTER" with 128 taps, 32-bit coefficients in 1.31 format, decimation ratio 2. [Established] [ADS127L21 data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/ads127l21.pdf)
- **TI PCM5122** audio DAC, data sheet SLAS763C, August 2012, revised October 2018; Electrical Characteristics at fS = 48 kHz (TA = 25 °C, 3.3 V supplies, 512 fS system clock, 24-bit data), 8× interpolation filters: normal: pass band 0.45 × fS, stop band 0.55 × fS, stop-band attenuation −60 dB (min), ripple ±0.02 dB, delay 20 × tS; low latency: 0.47 × fS, 0.55 × fS, −52 dB, ±0.0001 dB, 3.5 × tS; asymmetric FIR: 0.4 × fS, 0.72 × fS, −52 dB, ±0.05 dB, 1.2 × tS; high attenuation: pass band 0.45 × fS, stop band printed "0.45 × fS", −100 dB (typ), ±0.0005 dB, 33.7 × tS. Filter-select register names include "Low latency IIR interpolation filter" and "Ringing-less low latency FIR interpolation filter". **Internal inconsistencies:** the high-attenuation stop-band edge of 0.45 × fS conflicts with the detailed filter table (stop band from 0.55 × fS); the asymmetric filter's attenuation is −52 dB in the Electrical Characteristics but −50 dB in Tables 15–17. [Established] [PCM5122 data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/pcm5122.pdf)
- Perishable: data-sheet revisions can change typical values and correct the inconsistencies; re-read before citing.

## R-239 · FPGA DSP blocks for symmetric FIR filters (AMD DSP48E2/DSP58, Altera Agilex 7) and SciPy `remez`

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. AMD documents came from docs.amd.com; intel.com refused automated access (HTTP 403) and was not used, so the Altera guide was read on Altera's documentation portal. The UG579 pre-adder sentence was re-read by the main author from the converted PDF.*

- **AMD UG579**, "UltraScale Architecture DSP Slice", v1.11, 30 Aug 2021: "27 × 18 two's complement multiplier"; "Power saving 27-bit pre-adder: optimizes symmetrical filter applications and reduces DSP logic requirements"; "The most common use for the pre-adder is to pre-add corresponding values of a symmetric FIR filter tap delay line." Figure 4-6 shows an 8-tap even-symmetric systolic FIR in 4 DSP slices. [Established] [UG579](https://docs.amd.com/v/u/en-US/ug579-ultrascale-dsp)
- **AMD AM004**, "Versal ACAP DSP Engine Architecture Manual", rev. 1.2.1, 11 Sep 2022: "27 × 24 + 58 two's complement multiply-accumulator with 27-bit pre-addition"; "Power saving 27-bit pre-adder that optimizes symmetrical filter applications". [Established] [AM004](https://docs.amd.com/r/en-US/am004-versal-dsp-engine)
- **AMD UG949** (Vivado design methodology, 2025.2): for symmetric filters the dedicated pre-adder means "fewer DSP slices as well (in most cases, half the resources)". [Established] [UG949](https://docs.amd.com/r/2025.2-English/ug949-vivado-design-methodology/Coding-for-Optimal-DSP-and-Arithmetic-Inference)
- **Altera Agilex 7 Variable Precision DSP Blocks User Guide**, UG-20213, version 24.3.1, 6 Aug 2025: "Two 18 x 19 multipliers or one 27 x 27 multiplier per DSP block"; "Hard pre-adder supported in 18-bit and 27-bit DSP operation modes for symmetric filters"; "Internal coefficient register bank in both 18-bit and 27-bit modes"; "18-bit and 27-bit systolic finite impulse response (FIR) filters with distributed output adder". [Established] [Altera UG-20213](https://docs.altera.com/r/docs/683037/current)
- **SciPy `scipy.signal.remez`** documentation (SciPy v1.18.0 manual, stable in Nov 2026): "Calculate the minimax optimal filter using the Remez exchange algorithm"; references McClellan and Parks (1973). Observed locally with SciPy 1.18.1: `scipy.signal.kaiserord(60, width)` returns 176 taps for the 4–5 kHz, 48 kHz example, because its order formula uses A − 7.95 where Kaiser's (as in Oppenheim and Schafer) uses A − 8. [Established] [SciPy remez](https://docs.scipy.org/doc/scipy/reference/generated/scipy.signal.remez.html)
- Perishable: product generations and tool versions change; the pre-adder's role in symmetric FIR filters is stable.

## R-240 · Audio ADC biquads and decimation-filter latency options (TI TLV320ADC5140, SBAA378B)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (curl through the build proxy; garbled equations and tables were rendered as images to read them). The biquad count sentence and the Table 26 and Table 34 rows were re-read by the main author from the converted text.*

- **TI TLV320ADC5140** ("Quad-Channel, 768-kHz, Burr-Brown Audio ADC"), data sheet SBAS892A, July 2019, revised October 2019. §8.3.6.5: "The device supports up to 12 programmable digital biquad filters"; a biquad is "a second-order, recursive linear filter with two poles and two zeros"; Equation 2: H(z) = (N0 + 2N1 z⁻¹ + N2 z⁻²)/(2³¹ − 2D1 z⁻¹ − D2 z⁻²); default "flat at a gain of 0 dB (all-pass filter)". Table 18 (BIQUAD_CFG): 1 biquad per channel for all 8 channels, 2 per channel for up to 6 (default), 3 per channel for up to 4, or disabled. [Established] [TLV320ADC5140 data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/tlv320adc5140.pdf)
- **Decimation filters at 48 kHz** (§8.3.6.7, selected by DECI_FILT): linear phase (Table 26): pass-band ripple ±0.05 dB from 0 to 0.454 fS, stop-band attenuation 73.8 dB (0.58 to 4 fS) and 98.1 dB (from 4 fS), group delay 17.1/fS; low latency (Table 34): ±0.015 dB to 0.452 fS, 86.4 dB from 0.6 fS, group delay 7.7/fS over 0 to 0.365 fS, group-delay deviation ±0.027/fS, phase deviation −0.25° to 0.30°; ultra-low latency (Table 40): ±0.015 dB to 0.452 fS, 86.4 dB from 0.6 fS, group delay 4.1/fS over 0 to 0.325 fS, deviation ±0.525/fS, phase −10.3° to 14.5°. [Established]
- **TI SBAA378B** (application note on the device family's biquads), November 2019, revised December 2023: "The coefficients of these biquad filters [N0, N1, N2, D1, and D2] are each 32-bits wide, in 2s-complement format… the filter coefficients are in 1.31 format"; "the coefficients N1 and D1 are multiplied by two… coefficients D1 and D2 have a negative sign"; conversion N0 = b0, N1 = b1/2, N2 = b2, D1 = −a1/2, D2 = −a2; "The biquads are computed in 32-bit fixed point arithmetic." **Errors in the note:** it writes the 1.31 value of 1 as "2^–31 (0x7FFFFFF)" (it is 2³¹ − 1 = 0x7FFFFFFF in integer form), says both "truncate" and "round", and gives a first-order Butterworth slope as "–10 dB / decade" (it is 20 dB per decade). [Established] [SBAA378B](https://www.ti.com/lit/pdf/sbaa378)
- Perishable: data-sheet and note revisions change; re-read before citing.

## R-241 · Arm CMSIS-DSP biquad cascade functions and conventions

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the official documentation site (footer "Generated on Thu Sep 10 2026 … for CMSIS-DSP 1.18.0"). The coefficient-negation sentence was re-read by the main author from the saved page.*

- Function families: Direct Form I `arm_biquad_cascade_df1_{f16,f32,q15,q31}` (plus `_fast_q15`, `_fast_q31`); Direct Form II transposed `arm_biquad_cascade_df2T_{f16,f32,f64}` and stereo variants; "Only floating-point data is supported" for DF2T. [Established] [CMSIS-DSP Biquad DF1](https://arm-software.github.io/CMSIS-DSP/latest/group__BiquadCascadeDF1.html), [DF2T](https://arm-software.github.io/CMSIS-DSP/latest/group__BiquadCascadeDF2T.html)
- Difference equation: "y[n] = b0 * x[n] + b1 * x[n-1] + b2 * x[n-2] + a1 * y[n-1] + a2 * y[n-2]"; "Some design tools use the difference equation … - a1 * y[n-1] - a2 * y[n-2]. In this case the feedback coefficients a1 and a2 must be negated when used with the CMSIS DSP Library." Coefficients stored per stage as {b0, b1, b2, a1, a2} (Q15 adds a zero after b0 for SIMD). [Established]
- Fixed point: "coefficients are restricted to lie in the range [-1 +1). The fixed-point functions have an additional scaling parameter postShift … This essentially scales the filter coefficients by 2^postShift"; Direct Form I "is numerically more robust for fixed-point data types… transposed Direct Form II … requires a wide dynamic range for the state variables." [Established]
- **Accumulator and scaling statements, from the source-file doc comments** (added 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent; `ARM-software/CMSIS-DSP`, branch `main` at commit 9f64ba31591e527c505951943b4b95b0eb2f834b, identical at tag v1.18.0; `Source/FilteringFunctions/`, file headers dated 23 April 2021, V1.9.0):
  - `arm_biquad_cascade_df1_q15.c`: "The 2.30 intermediate results are accumulated in a 64-bit accumulator in 34.30 format. There is no risk of internal overflow with this approach and the full precision of intermediate multiplications is preserved."
  - `arm_biquad_cascade_df1_q31.c`: "The function is implemented using an internal 64-bit accumulator. The accumulator has a 2.62 format and maintains full precision of the intermediate multiplication results but provides only a single guard bit. Thus, if the accumulator result overflows it wraps around rather than clip"; the input "must be scaled down by 2 bits and lie in the range [-0.25 +0.25)".
  - `arm_biquad_cascade_df1_fast_q15.c`: a "32-bit accumulator with 2.30 format" that "provides only a single guard bit"; `arm_biquad_cascade_df1_fast_q31.c`: products "truncated to 2.30 format" and "added to a 2.30 accumulator", with the same [−0.25, +0.25) input requirement. [Established] [Source files](https://github.com/ARM-software/CMSIS-DSP/tree/9f64ba31591e527c505951943b4b95b0eb2f834b/Source/FilteringFunctions)
- Perishable: library versions and function sets change.

## R-242 · W3C Audio EQ Cookbook and Web Audio BiquadFilterNode; SciPy's second-order-sections recommendation

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. The SciPy 'sos' sentence was re-read by the main author from the saved page.*

- **W3C Audio EQ Cookbook**, Working Group Note, 8 June 2021, editor Raymond Toy; "Adapted from Audio-EQ-Cookbook.txt, by Robert Bristow-Johnson, with permission"; filters "were derived from analog prototypes … digitized using the Bilinear Transform (BLT)" with prewarping; ω0 = 2π f0/Fs, α = sin ω0/(2Q); low-pass b0 = (1 − cos ω0)/2, b1 = 1 − cos ω0, b2 = (1 − cos ω0)/2, a0 = 1 + α, a1 = −2 cos ω0, a2 = 1 − α. [Established] [Audio EQ Cookbook](https://www.w3.org/TR/audio-eq-cookbook/)
- **W3C Web Audio API**: Recommendation, 17 June 2021; version 1.1 a Working Draft dated 22 September 2026. `BiquadFilterType`: "lowpass", "highpass", "bandpass", "lowshelf", "highshelf", "peaking", "notch", "allpass"; §1.13.5: "The formulas in this section describe the filters that a conforming implementation MUST implement… They are inspired by formulas found in the Audio EQ Cookbook." Lowpass and highpass treat Q in decibels (α = sin ω0/(2·10^(Q/20))). [Established] [Web Audio API](https://www.w3.org/TR/webaudio/)
- **SciPy v1.18.0** (`iirdesign`, `iirfilter`): "In general the second-order sections ('sos') form is recommended because inferring the coefficients for the numerator/denominator form ('ba') suffers from numerical instabilities. For reasons of backward compatibility the default form is the numerator/denominator form ('ba')"; `ellip`: "the DC gain is unity for odd-order filters, or -rp dB for even-order filters". [Established] [SciPy iirdesign](https://docs.scipy.org/doc/scipy/reference/generated/scipy.signal.iirdesign.html)
- Perishable: specification status and software defaults change.

- **TI ADS127L11**, data sheet SBAS946C (revised September 2022), §8.3.5.2: "The low-latency filter is a cascaded-integrator-comb (CIC) topology that minimizes the delay (latency)… The CIC filter is otherwise known as a sinc filter because of the characteristic sinx/x (sinc) frequency response"; choices "sinc4, sinc4 + sinc1, sinc3, and sinc3 + sinc1"; Equation 17 gives the response with stage-1 OSR A, stage-2 OSR B, order n of 3 or 4 and fMOD = fCLK/2 = 12.8 MHz in high-speed mode. Table 8-5 (sinc4, high-speed mode, fCLK = 25.6 MHz): OSR 32: 400 kSPS, −3 dB frequency 91.0 kHz, latency 10.63 µs; OSR 64: 200 kSPS, 45.5 kHz, 20.63 µs; OSR 128: 100 kSPS, 22.75 kHz, 40.63 µs. ## R-244 · Hogenauer's CIC paper: bibliographic record

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. IEEE Xplore returned an empty bot-protection response and was not worked around; the record is IEEE's own metadata as deposited with Crossref, and the abstract sentence comes from a secondary index (OpenAlex), not checked against Xplore.*

- E. B. Hogenauer, "An economical class of digital filters for decimation and interpolation", *IEEE Transactions on Acoustics, Speech, and Signal Processing* 29(2), 155–162, April 1981, DOI 10.1109/TASSP.1981.1163535 (Crossref). Abstract (secondary): "They require no multipliers and use limited storage making them an economical alternative to conventional implementations for certain applications"; "A digital filter in this class consists of cascaded ideal integrator stages operating at a high sampling rate and an equal number of comb stages operating at a low sampling rate." ## R-248 · OCP 8-bit floating point (OFP8): E4M3 and E5M2

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (PDFs from opencompute.org); the two maximum-value sentences were re-read by the main author from the converted text.*

- **OCP 8-bit Floating Point Specification (OFP8)**, Revision 1.0 approved 20 June 2023 (corrected 1 December 2023: "Corrects the biases for E4M3 and E5M2 in Section 4.2"), contributors "AMD, Arm, Google, Intel, Meta, and NVIDIA"; Revision 1.1 dated 29 January 2026 ("Adjusts the conversion description in Section 5.2 and adds Appendix A"), adding AWS as a contributor. Tables 1 and 2 (unchanged in 1.1): E4M3 bias 7, emax 8, maximum normal S.1111.110 = ±448, minimum subnormal ±2⁻⁹, no infinities, NaN S.1111.111; E5M2 bias 15, maximum ±57,344, infinities S.11111.00, NaN S.11111.{01,10,11}. "max_E4M3: The maximum magnitude of a number in the E4M3 format. It has the value 448." "The E4M3 format does not represent infinities and uses only two bit patterns for NaN … in order to increase emax to 8." Conversion: round to nearest, ties to even; saturating and non-saturating modes. [Established] [OFP8 rev 1.1](https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-8-bit-floating-point-specification-ofp8-revision-1-1-final-pdf/)

## R-249 · OCP Microscaling (MX) formats

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent; the shared-scale sentence was re-read by the main author from the converted v1.0 text.*

- **OCP Microscaling Formats (MX) Specification v1.0**, September 2023 (version table 9/7/2023), contributors "AMD, Arm, Intel, Meta, Microsoft, NVIDIA, Qualcomm". Table 1: block size k = 32 with an 8-bit E8M0 scale for every format; elements MXFP8 (E5M2 or E4M3, 8 bits), MXFP6 (E3M2 or E2M3, 6 bits), MXFP4 (E2M1, 4 bits), MXINT8 (8 bits). §5.1: "The scale factor X is shared across all k elements. The data types of the elements and scale are chosen independently"; "each block of k elements can be encoded in (w + kd) bits." §5.4.1: "E8M0 is an unsigned representation of a conventional biased Float32 exponent." [Established] [MX v1.0](https://opencompute.org/documents/ocp-microscaling-formats-mx-v1-0-spec-final-pdf)
- **v1.1** (dated 9 October 2026, retitled "OCP MicroXcaling (MX) Specification", Google added as a contributor) renames E8M0 to UE8M0 and rewrites the scale-selection algorithm (§6.3); Table 1 is otherwise unchanged. Its approval status was not found on an OCP page. [Established for the document's content] [MX v1.1](https://www.opencompute.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/OCP_MicroXcaling_Spec_v1.1.0.pdf)

## R-250 · IEEE 754-2019 status and binary16 parameters

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. The standard's text is paywalled and was not opened; an unauthorized copy found by search was deliberately not used.*

- IEEE SA: IEEE 754-2019 "Status: Active Standard" (approved 2019-06-13, published 2019-07-22, superseding 754-2008); revision project P754 "Status: Active PAR; PAR Approval 2024-06-06; Superseding 754-2019". [Established] [IEEE 754-2019](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/754/6210/), [P754](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/754/11684/)
- binary16 parameters (secondary): the ISO C working draft N3220, Annex H, Table H.1, gives precision p = 11 bits for binary16, with C-model emax 16 and a footnote that the C model's exponent is one more than the ISO/IEC 60559 model's, so IEEE emax = 15; NumPy's float16 agrees (maximum 65,504). [Established] [N3220](https://www.open-std.org/jtc1/sc22/wg14/www/docs/n3220.pdf)

## R-251 · bfloat16 definitions

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent.*

- Google Cloud blog, "BFloat16: The secret to high performance on Cloud TPUs" (23 August 2019): bfloat16 is "comprised of one sign bit, eight exponent bits, and seven mantissa bits"; "the dynamic range of bfloat16 is identical to that of FP32." OCP OFP8 §4.1: "bfloat16: a binary format with 1 sign bit, 8 exponent bits, 7 mantissa bits, and an exponent bias of 127." Intel, "BFLOAT16 – Hardware Numerics Definition" (Rev 1.0, Nov 2018): "a short version of FP32, skipping the least significant 16 bits of mantissa." [Established] [Google Cloud blog](https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/ai-machine-learning/bfloat16-the-secret-to-high-performance-on-cloud-tpus)

## R-252 · TI C7x DSP and matrix-multiply accelerator

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (curl through the build proxy); the 512-bit and 4096-multiplication sentences and the TDA4VM feature line were re-read by the main author from the converted text.*

- **TI SPRUIP0**, "C71x DSP CPU, Instruction Set, and Matrix Multiply Accelerator" (May 2021): "The first implementation of the C7x platform is a full-feature with 512-bit vector width CPU core"; "Dual data path with one 64-bit scalar side (Side A) and one 512-bit vector side (Side B)"; "traditional C6x VLIW DSP operating mode"; Table 3-1 (C7x vector side against C66x): fixed-point 16 × 16 MACs per cycle 32 → 128, 32 × 32 8 → 32, single-precision floating-point MACs 8 → 32, vector size 128 → 512 bits. MMA: "For 8-bit operands, there are 64 elements in row vector A and 4096 elements in square matrix B, resulting in a requirement for 4096 multiplications and the equivalent of 4096 summations per clock cycle" (1024 for 16-bit, 256 for 32-bit operands). [Established] [SPRUIP0](https://www.ti.com/lit/pdf/spruip0)
- **TI TDA4VM** data sheet SPRSP36L (revised June 2026): "C7x floating point, vector DSP, up to 1.0GHz, 80 GFLOPS, 256 GOPS"; "Deep-learning matrix multiply accelerator (MMA), up to 8 TOPS (8b) at 1.0GHz". Derived (ours): 128 × 2 × 1 GHz = 256 GOPS and 4096 × 2 × 1 GHz = 8.19 TOPS, consistent with both. TI's C7000 optimization guide (v4.1, 2024) calls the core "Very-Long-Instruction-Word (VLIW)". Smaller parts (AM62A7, TDA4AEN-Q1) use a 256-bit vector C7x at "up to 40 GFLOPS". [Established] [TDA4VM data sheet](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/tda4vm.pdf)

## R-256 · Practitioner citations for Course 16: EQ processing modes, selectable 50/60 Hz rejection, real-time spectrum analysis, partitioned convolution

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (curl), during the Course 16 audit. tek.com and the AES e-library served Cloudflare challenges and analog.com refused connections; none was worked around.*

- **FabFilter Pro-Q 4 online help, "Processing mode"** (page shows © 2026, no version date): "In Zero Latency mode, Pro-Q matches the magnitude response of analog EQing as closely as possible, obviously without introducing any latency"; "Higher processing resolution (for better response in the low frequencies), results in longer latency, but unfortunately this can also introduce 'pre-ring' that can make transients (e.g. a kick drum) lose their edge." Linear-phase latencies at 44.1 kHz from 3072 samples (about 70 ms, Low) to 66,560 samples (about 1.5 s, Maximum). FabFilter's article "When to use a linear phase EQ?": "Most equalizers used today are called minimum phase filters". [Established] [Processing mode](https://www.fabfilter.com/help/pro-q/using/processingmode) · [Linear phase EQ](https://www.fabfilter.com/learn/equalization/linear-phase-eq/)
- **TI ADS1220** 24-bit ADC, data sheet SBAS501D (May 2013, revised May 2026), §8.3.6: "At data rates of 5SPS and 20SPS, use the 50/60[1:0] bits in the configuration register to select line frequency rejections of 50Hz or 60Hz or to simultaneously reject 50Hz and 60Hz"; normal-mode rejection at 20 SPS at least 105 dB at 50 Hz or 60 Hz ±3% and 90 dB with simultaneous rejection. A biopotential (ECG) example was not obtained. [Established] [ADS1220](https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/ads1220.pdf)
- **Rohde & Schwarz white paper 1EF77_3e**, F. Ramian, "Implementation of Real-Time Spectrum Analysis" (3.2015): "Parallel sampling and FFT calculation: The data acquisition continues while the FFTs are performed"; "Overlapping 'reuses' samples that were already used to calculate the preceding FFT result"; in default operation the R&S real-time analyzers "operate with an overlap of more than 50%, i.e. they have no blind time." [Established] [1EF77](https://scdn.rohde-schwarz.com/ur/pws/dl_downloads/dl_application/application_notes/1ef77/1EF77_3e_Real-time_Spectrum_Analysis.pdf)
- **Partitioned convolution:** W. G. Gardner, "Efficient Convolution without Input-Output Delay", *J. Audio Eng. Soc.* 43(3), 127–136, March 1995, as cited by US patent 6,625,629 B1 (Google Patents, non-patent citations) and by Battenberg and Avizienis, DAFx-11, ref. [9]; no DOI found; the AES record itself was not reachable. The 1994 convention preprint (AES 97th Convention, paper 3897) abstract, seen only in a search snippet: "block processing incurs significant input/output delay which is undesirable for real-time applications". [Established, secondary bibliographic sources]

## R-257 · Spectrum-analyzer noise measurement (Keysight UXA, R&S FSW) and the kT0 reference

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the documents themselves (curl and pdftotext). Keysight's application notes AN 150 and AN 57-1 are now behind a registration form and were not read; MathWorks documentation refused automated access.*

- **Keysight N9041B UXA Signal Analyzer Specifications Guide** (N9041-90004, Edition 1, Feb 2024): resolution-filter "Noise BW to RBW ratio 1.056 ±2% (nominal)"; "The noise marker, band power marker, channel power and ACP all compute their results using the power bandwidth of the RBW used for the measurement"; "DANL is, by Keysight and industry practice, specified with log averaging, which reduces the measured noise level by 2.51 dB ... noise bandwidth of 1.056 Hz. Therefore, the noise density is 2.27 dB above the DANL"; in its noise-figure formula, kTB is "−173.98 dBm in a 1 Hz bandwidth at 290 K". (One footnote elsewhere in the same guide gives 2.25 dB, inconsistent with 2.51 − 0.24 = 2.27; the main text's 2.27 is used.) [Established] [UXA specifications](https://www.keysight.com/us/en/assets/9018-05001/technical-specifications/9018-05001.pdf)
- **Rohde & Schwarz FSW User Manual** (1173.9411.02, version 57, 2026), §8.3.4.2 "Measuring noise density": "Noise density is the noise referred to a bandwidth of 1 Hz"; the correction is "10 x lg (1 Hz/BWNoise)" with BWNoise the noise bandwidth of the RBW filter; with the RMS detector "With the exception of bandwidth correction, no further corrections are required"; with the sample detector "1.05 dB is added to the marker level. This is the difference between the average value and the RMS value of white noise", and "With a logarithmic level axis, 1.45 dB is added additionally"; with the RMS detector, trace averaging "produces noise levels that are too low and cannot be corrected"; a narrow logarithmic video bandwidth gives "a too low indication of the power (-2.51 dB at very low video bandwidths)". [Established] [FSW manual](https://scdn.rohde-schwarz.com/ur/pws/dl_downloads/pdm/cl_manuals/user_manual/1173_9411_01/FSW_UserManual_en_57.pdf)
- **Keysight 89600 VSA help, "Noise Measurements"**: "Averaging a noise-like signal on a log scale can cause an error as great as -2.51 dB." [Established]
- **ITU-R P.372-18** (09/2026), §1.2.1: "T0 : reference temperature (K) taken as 290 K". CODATA: k = 1.380 649 × 10⁻²³ J/K, exact. Derived: kT0 = 4.0039 × 10⁻²¹ W/Hz = −173.98 dBm/Hz. [Established] [ITU-R P.372-18](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/p/R-REC-P.372-18-202609-I!!PDF-E.pdf)
- Derived (ours, checked in verify_17_01.py by simulation): the Rayleigh envelope's mean is √(π/4) of its rms (−1.049 dB); log-averaging an exponentially distributed power reads low by 10 log10(e)·γ = 2.507 dB; their difference, 1.458 dB, is the R&S 1.45 dB.

## R-258 · 3GPP NR adjacent channel leakage ratio (TS 38.104, TS 38.101-1)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP specification archives (zip files of the published versions). Values were cross-checked against the Release-15 versions, which agree.*

- **TS 38.104 V20.1.0 (2026-09), base station:** ACLR is "the ratio of the filtered mean power centred on the assigned channel frequency to the filtered mean power centred on an adjacent channel frequency"; FR1 conducted (Table 6.6.3.2-1): 45 dB at the first and second adjacent NR channels (38 dB for band n104); absolute basic limits (Table 6.6.3.2-2): −13 dBm/MHz (Category A wide area), −15 (Category B wide area), −25 (medium range), −32 (local area), applied "whichever is less stringent"; FR2 (Table 9.7.3.3-1): 28 dB at 24.25–33.4 GHz, 26 dB at 37–52.6 GHz, 24 dB at 52.6–71 GHz (the last not in Release 15). [Established] [38.104 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.104/38104-k10.zip)
- **TS 38.101-1 V20.1.0 (2026-09), UE:** NR ACLR (Table 6.5.2.4.1-2): power class 1: 37 dB, 1.5: 31 dB, 2: 31 dB, 3: 30 dB (26 dB in n104), applying when the adjacent-channel power exceeds −50 dBm; UTRA ACLR1 33 dB and ACLR2 36 dB with a 3.84 MHz RRC (roll-off 0.22) filter; §6.5.2.4: the result "should be integrated over the measurement bandwidth in order to obtain the equivalent noise bandwidth of the measurement bandwidth." [Established] [38.101-1 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.101-1/38101-1-k10.zip)

## R-259 · GPS L1 C/A minimum received power (IS-GPS-200N)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 from the interface specification itself (gps.gov PDF, converted with pdftotext). The interface revision notices IRN-IS-200N-003 (19 Jan 2024) and IRN-IS-200N-004 (16 Jun 2026) were also read; neither changes Table 3-V.*

- **IS-GPS-200N (1 Aug 2022), Table 3-Va** ("Received Minimum RF Signal Strength for Block IIR, IIR-M, IIF, GPS III, and GPS IIIF Satellites", 20.46 MHz bandwidth): L1 C/A −158.5 dBW (−128.5 dBm), L1 P(Y) −161.5 dBW; the level is "measured at the output of a 3 dBi linearly polarized user receiving antenna (located near ground) at worst normal orientation, when the SV is above a 5-degree elevation angle". Carriers: "The nominal carrier frequencies (f0) shall be 1575.42 MHz, and 1227.6 MHz for L1 and L2"; the C/A code is a "1023 BIT Gold Code @ 1023 kbps" with a 1 ms period. [Established] [IS-GPS-200N](https://www.gps.gov/sites/default/files/2025-07/IS-GPS-200N.pdf)

## R-260 · 3GPP NR reference sensitivity and its noise-figure assumptions

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent and re-checked against the local copies of the published specification archives (zip/docx).*

- **TR 38.817-02 V15.12.0, §7.2.1 (base station):** reference sensitivity follows $P_{\text{REFSENS}} = -174 + 10\log_{10}(\text{BW}) + \text{NF} + \text{IM} + \text{SNR}$, where "NF is BS noise figure, equal to 5 dB for Wide Area BS, 10 dB for Medium Range BS and 13 dB for Local Area BS" and "IM is the implementation margin, equal to 2dB"; SNR is the value "for which we reach 95% throughput". [Established]
- **TR 38.817-01 V16.4.0, §6.2 (UE):** "Sensitivity = -174dBm(kT) + 10*log(RX BW) + NF + SNR +IM – diversity gain"; SNR −1 dB; "If 2Rx is considered, the diversity gain is 3dB and the Implementation Margin (IM) uses 2.5dB"; for bands n77–n79 "the agreed NR values are 10.5dB, 10dB and 10dB". [Established] UE REFSENS itself (TS 38.101-1 V20.1.0, §7.3.2) requires throughput "≥ 95 % of the maximum throughput of the reference measurement channels" of Annex A.3.2; Table A.3.2.2-1 ("Fixed reference channel for receiver requirements (SCS 15 kHz, FDD, QPSK 1/3)") specifies QPSK, target coding rate 1/3 and "Maximum number of HARQ transmissions" 1 (re-read 21 Nov 2026 from the local copy of the 38.101-1 archive). [Established]
- **TS 38.104 V20.1.0 (2026-09), Table 7.2.2-1:** wide-area BS REFSENS −101.7 dBm for 5–15 MHz channels at 15 kHz with reference channel G-FR1-A1-1, which Table A.1-1 defines as 25 resource blocks, QPSK, code rate 1/3. [Established] [38.104 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.104/38104-k10.zip)
- **TS 38.101-1 V20.1.0 (2026-09), Table 7.3.2-1b:** two-port REFSENS for n78: −95.8 + 10log10(NRB/52) dBm at 15 kHz, −96.1 + 10log10(NRB/24) at 30 kHz, −96.5 + 10log10(NRB/11) at 60 kHz; note 1: four receive ports are the baseline for this band (two-port values are adjusted by Table 7.3.2-2). Derived (ours): the TR 38.817-01 equation with NF 10 dB reproduces −95.79 dBm for 52 RB at 15 kHz. [Established] [38.101-1 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.101-1/38101-1-k10.zip)

## R-261 · Wi-Fi (802.11ac) receiver minimum sensitivity

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 from a secondary source: the IEEE 802.11 standard itself is behind a paywall and was not read. Rohde & Schwarz reproduces the standard's table; its 802.11ax white paper (3609.9470.52, v01.00, Apr 2020) extends it to 1024-QAM.*

- **Rohde & Schwarz, "802.11ac Technology Introduction", application note 1MA192, 7e (March 2012), Table 11** ("Receiver Minimum Sensitivity Specification", 4096-byte packets, PER < 10%): at 20 MHz, BPSK 1/2 −82 dBm, QPSK 1/2 −79, QPSK 3/4 −77, 16-QAM 1/2 −74, 16-QAM 3/4 −70, 64-QAM 2/3 −66, 64-QAM 3/4 −65, 64-QAM 5/6 −64, 256-QAM 3/4 −59, 256-QAM 5/6 −57 dBm; each doubling of bandwidth adds 3 dB (40, 80, 160 MHz columns). The 802.11ax white paper's Table 19 repeats these rows and adds 1024-QAM 3/4 −54 dBm and 5/6 −52 dBm at 20 MHz. [Established, secondary source]

## R-263 · US FM broadcast rules (47 CFR Part 73)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the eCFR point-in-time XML (as of 18 Nov 2026) and re-checked against the saved text.*

- **§ 73.310(a):** "frequency deviation of ±75kHz is defined as 100% modulation"; an FM broadcast channel is "A band of frequencies 200 kHz wide", from 88.1 MHz "in successive steps of 200 kHz to and including 107.9 MHz". **§ 73.201:** the band "between 88 MHz and 108 MHz ... is divided into 100 channels of 200 kHz each". [Established] [eCFR Part 73](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-C/part-73)
- **§ 73.317:** (b) emissions 120–240 kHz from the carrier "must be attenuated at least 25 dB below the level of the unmodulated carrier"; (e) "Preemphasis shall not be greater than the impedance-frequency characteristics of a series inductance resistance network having a time constant of 75 microseconds" (a ceiling, not a fixed value). [Established]
- **§ 73.322 (stereo):** "The pilot subcarrier at 19 kHz ±2 Hz, must frequency modulate the main carrier between the limits of 8 and 10 percent"; the stereophonic subcarrier is the second harmonic (38 kHz); "Double sideband, suppressed-carrier, amplitude modulation of the stereophonic subcarrier at 38 kHz must be used", suppressed "to a level less than 1% modulation"; its modulating signal is L − R; the subcarrier's baseband range is 23 kHz to 53 kHz. [Established]

## R-264 · ITU-R BS.450-4, FM sound broadcasting at VHF

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the ITU PDF (pdftotext dropped the ± and µ symbols, restored here).*

- **Rec. ITU-R BS.450-4 (10/2019), in force:** maximum frequency deviation ±75 kHz or ±50 kHz; NOTE 1: "In the West European countries and the United States of America, the maximum deviation is ±75 kHz. In the ex-USSR and in some other European countries, it is 50 kHz"; NOTE 2: "In Europe, the pre-emphasis is 50 µs. In the United States of America, it is 75 µs"; the pilot-tone stereo system "has become the de facto worldwide standard", with M = (A + B)/2, S = (A − B)/2, a 38 kHz ±4 Hz subcarrier and a 19 kHz pilot at 8–10%. [Established] [BS.450-4](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/bs/R-REC-BS.450-4-201910-I!!PDF-E.pdf)

[Established]

- **PCIe 7.0 release** (PCI-SIG press release, 11 Jun 2025, reprinted by Signal Integrity Journal): "official release of the PCI Express® (PCIe®) 7.0 specification, reaching 128.0 GT/s, to members"; "Utilizes PAM4 (Pulse Amplitude Modulation with 4 levels) signaling and Flit-based encoding". [Established, secondary]
- **PCIe 8.0 announcement** (PCI-SIG press release, 5 Aug 2025, reprinted by Silicon UK): 256.0 GT/s, "planned for release to members by 2028". [Established, secondary]
- **PCIe 6.0/7.0 PHY** (D. Das Sharma, SNIA SDC slides): "PAM4 Signaling at 64.0 and 128.0 GT/s ... 3 eyes ... Helps channel loss (same Nyquist as 32.0 GT/s) ... Gray Coding ... Precoding"; flit "256B ... 236B TLP, 6B DLP, 8B CRC, 6B FEC"; "Light-weight FEC, strong CRC". [Established, secondary] [slides](https://www.snia.org/sites/default/files/2025-05/SNIA-SDC23-DasSharma-PCIe-7.0-Specification-Bandwidth.pdf)

The measurement criterion (PER or BER) for the sensitivity is not stated in the table. [Established]

## R-276 · DVB-S2 performance table and roll-offs (ETSI EN 302 307)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the ETSI PDFs; V1.4.1 of part 1 is the only version on the ETSI server.*

- **EN 302 307-1 V1.4.1 (2014-11), Table 13**, "Es/No performance at Quasi Error Free PER = 10-7 (AWGN channel)", normal FECFRAME 64 800, ideal Es/N0 with spectral efficiency (bits per symbol): QPSK 1/4 −2.35 dB (0.490243), QPSK 1/2 1.00 dB (0.988858), QPSK 3/4 4.03 dB (1.487473), 8PSK 2/3 6.62 dB (1.980636), 16APSK 3/4 10.21 dB (2.966728), 32APSK 9/10 16.05 dB (4.453027); the note gives "Eb/N0 = Es/N0 - 10log10(ηtot)". §5.6: "square root raised cosine filtered. The roll-off factor shall be α = 0,35, 0,25 and 0,20". [Established] [ETSI EN 302 307-1](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_en/302300_302399/30230701/01.04.01_60/en_30230701v010401p.pdf)
- **EN 302 307-2 V1.4.1 (2024-08)** (DVB-S2X), §5.6, adds roll-offs 0.15, 0.10 and 0.05. Its performance tables use FER = 10⁻⁵ and are not directly comparable with part 1's. [Established]

## R-278 · Bluetooth LE PHY test conditions (secondary)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. bluetooth.com refused automated access, so the Core Specification was not read; the facts below come from a copy of the Bluetooth SIG's RF-PHY test suite (RF-PHY.TS.5.1.1, 2019-08-01) hosted by Infineon and from Silicon Labs and TI documentation. Secondary.*

- The LE 1M receiver-sensitivity test injects −70 dBm and passes below 30.8% packet-error rate for 37-byte payloads; the LE Coded PHY runs at 500 kb/s (S = 2) and 125 kb/s (S = 8), tested at −75 and −82 dBm; the modulation index of the test transmitter ranges from 0.45 to 0.55 and the deviation test requires 225 kHz ≤ Δf1avg ≤ 275 kHz. Silicon Labs states "Minimum RX sensitivity -70 dBm (BER 0.1%)". [Established, secondary]

## R-279 · 3GPP NR channel coding (TS 38.212)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP archive (38212-j40.zip) and the identical ETSI PDF (TS 138 212 V19.4.0), whose equations extract as text; key passages re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **TS 38.212 V19.4.0 (2026-06), Release 19, clause 5.3, Tables 5.3-1 and 5.3-2:** UL-SCH, DL-SCH and PCH use LDPC; BCH and DCI use polar codes; UCI uses a block code or a polar code. [Established] [38.212 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.212/38212-j40.zip)
- **Clause 5.1:** six CRC generator polynomials: gCRC24A(D) = D^24 + D^23 + D^18 + D^17 + D^14 + D^11 + D^10 + D^7 + D^6 + D^5 + D^4 + D^3 + D + 1; gCRC24B(D) = D^24 + D^23 + D^6 + D^5 + D + 1; gCRC24C(D) = D^24 + D^23 + D^21 + D^20 + D^17 + D^15 + D^13 + D^12 + D^8 + D^4 + D^2 + D + 1; gCRC16(D) = D^16 + D^12 + D^5 + 1; gCRC11(D) = D^11 + D^10 + D^9 + D^5 + 1; gCRC6(D) = D^6 + D^5 + 1. Clauses 6.2.1/7.2.1: UL-SCH and DL-SCH transport blocks use CRC24A "if A > 3824" and CRC16 otherwise; clause 5.2.2: each LDPC code block after segmentation gets a 24-bit CRC with gCRC24B; clauses 7.1.3 and 7.3.2: BCH and DCI use CRC24C; clause 6.3.1.2.1: polar-coded UCI uses CRC6 for 12 ≤ A ≤ 19 and CRC11 for A ≥ 20. [Established] [ETSI TS 138 212](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/138200_138299/138212/19.04.00_60/ts_138212v190400p.pdf)
- **Clauses 5.2.2 and 5.3.2, Table 5.3.2-1:** maximum code block Kcb = 8448 (base graph 1) and 3840 (base graph 2); codeword length N = 66·Zc (BG1) and 50·Zc (BG2); base matrices of 46 × 68 (BG1) and 42 × 52 (BG2), each 1 replaced by a Zc × Zc circularly shifted identity; lifting sizes in eight sets up to Zc = 384. Clause 6.2.2: base graph 2 "if A ≤ 292, or if A ≤ 3824 and R ≤ 0.67, or if R ≤ 0.25", otherwise base graph 1. Clause 5.3.1.2: polar sequence for Nmax = 1024. Derived (ours): 8448 = 22 × 384 and 3840 = 10 × 384; lowest mother-code rates 22/66 = 1/3 and 10/50 = 1/5. [Established]
- **Polar mother-code length by channel (ETSI TS 138 212 V19.4.0, 2026-07, re-read 21 Nov 2026 from the saved PDF):** clause 7.1.4 (BCH) and clause 7.3.3 (DCI) encode "via Polar coding according to Clause 5.3.1, by setting nmax = 9" (mother code N ≤ 512); clause 6.3.1.3 (UCI) sets "nmax = 10" (N ≤ 1024). [Established]

## R-280 · Ethernet "KP4" Reed–Solomon FEC, RS(544,514)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from public ieee802.org documents; the published IEEE 802.3 standard is paywalled and was not read. Re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **IEEE Draft P802.3bs/D3.1 (30 Jan 2017), Clause 119 (200GBASE-R and 400GBASE-R PCS):** "The PCS shall implement an RS(544,514) based FEC encoder"; "α is a primitive element of the finite field defined by the polynomial x10+x3+1"; "The Reed-Solomon decoder shall be capable of correcting any combination of up to t=15 symbol errors in a codeword." [Established, draft text] [gustlin_3bs_03_0317](https://www.ieee802.org/3/bs/public/17_03/gustlin_3bs_03_0317.pdf)
- **Cideciyan, Gustlin, Li, Wang, Wang, "Next Generation Backplane and Copper Cable Challenges", *IEEE Communications Magazine*, Dec 2013:** the NRZ PHY (100GBASE-KR4/CR4) uses "an RS(528, 514, t = 7, m = 10) code", the PAM-4 PHY "an RS(544, 514, t = 15, m = 10) code". [Established] [copy on ieee802.org](https://www.ieee802.org/3/400GSG/email/pdf7vtAkT89g3.pdf)
- **Gustlin and Lusted, "802.3bj FEC Overview and Status", IEEE P802.3db task force ad hoc (June 2020, from the file name):** 802.3bj defines both RS(528,514) and RS(544,514); 802.3bs uses RS(544,514) (Clause 119); 802.3cd, 802.3ck and 802.3cu require RS(544,514); "RS(544,514) used for 50/100G per lane technology / RS(528,514) is used for 25G technology"; RS(544,514) is "aka 'KP4'". [Established, secondary (task-force slides)] [gustlin_3db_adhoc_01_062520](https://www.ieee802.org/3/db/public/adhoc/presentations/gustlin_3db_adhoc_01_062520.pdf)

## R-281 · The rate-1/2, K = 7 convolutional code (CCSDS; 802.11)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent; re-checked against the saved CCSDS text on 21 Nov 2026. The IEEE 802.11 text is paywalled and was not read.*

- **CCSDS 131.0-B-5, "TM Synchronization and Channel Coding", Blue Book, Sept 2023, §3.3.1:** "The basic convolutional code shall be the non-systematic code with the following characteristics: (1) Nomenclature: Convolutional code with maximum-likelihood decoding. (2) Code rate (r): 1/2 bit per symbol. (3) Constraint length (K): 7 bits. (4) Connection vectors: G1 = 1111001 (171 octal); G2 = 1011011 (133 octal). (5) Symbol inversion: On output path of G2." The same vectors define the punctured codes of §3.4. [Established] [CCSDS 131.0-B-5](https://ccsds.org/Pubs/131x0b5.pdf)
- **Yan and Dai, arXiv:2502.15475 (2025), §2.1.1:** IEEE 802.11's convolutional encoder has generators g0 = 133₈ and g1 = 171₈, "a mother code rate of 1/2 and a constraint length of 7", punctured to rates 2/3, 3/4 and 5/6. The output ordering in the 802.11 text itself was not verified. [Established, secondary] [arXiv:2502.15475](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.15475)
- **CCSDS 131.0-B-5, table of contents (saved text, re-read 21 Nov 2026):** besides the convolutional and Reed–Solomon codes, the Blue Book specifies turbo coding (section 6) and LDPC coding of a transfer frame (section 7: a rate-223/255 code, §7.3, and a family with rates 1/2, 2/3 and 4/5, §7.4) and of a stream of sync-marked frames (section 8). [Established]

## R-282 · ITU-R P.525-5 free-space attenuation

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the ITU PDF; re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **Recommendation ITU-R P.525-5 (11/2024), "Calculation of free-space attenuation"**, in force, superseding P.525-4 (08/2019). Annex 1, eq. (5): $L_{bf} = 20\log_{10}(4\pi d/\lambda)$ dB with $d$ and λ in the same unit; eq. (6): "$L_{bf}$ = 32.4 + 20 log10 f + 20 log10 d dB" with f in MHz and d in km. Derived (ours): the exact constant is $20\log_{10}(4\pi \times 10^9/c)$ = 32.45 dB. [Established] [P.525-5](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/p/R-REC-P.525-5-202411-I!!PDF-E.pdf)

## R-283 · 3GPP TR 38.901 path loss and shadowing

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP archive (38901-j50.zip; its OMML equations extract cleanly); the breakpoint note and UMi rows re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **TR 38.901 V19.5.0 (2026-09), Release 19**, scope "0.5-100 GHz". **Table 7.4.1-1** ("fc is in GHz and d is in meters"): UMa LOS $PL_1 = 28.0 + 22\log_{10}d_{3D} + 20\log_{10}f_c$ (σ_SF = 4); UMa NLOS $\max(PL_{LOS}, 13.54 + 39.08\log_{10}d_{3D} + 20\log_{10}f_c - 0.6(h_{UT} - 1.5))$ (σ_SF = 6), optional $32.4 + 20\log_{10}f_c + 30\log_{10}d_{3D}$ (σ_SF = 7.8); UMi-Street Canyon LOS $PL_1 = 32.4 + 21\log_{10}d_{3D} + 20\log_{10}f_c$ for $10\,\text{m} \le d_{2D} \le d'_{BP}$ (σ_SF = 4, $h_{BS}$ = 10 m); UMi NLOS $\max(PL_{LOS}, 35.3\log_{10}d_{3D} + 22.4 + 21.3\log_{10}f_c - 0.3(h_{UT} - 1.5))$ (σ_SF = 7.82), optional $32.4 + 20\log_{10}f_c + 31.9\log_{10}d_{3D}$ (σ_SF = 8.2); InH LOS $32.4 + 17.3\log_{10}d_{3D} + 20\log_{10}f_c$ (σ_SF = 3), NLOS σ_SF = 8.03 (optional 8.29); RMa σ_SF = 4/6 (LOS) and 8 (NLOS). **Note 1:** "Effective breakpoint distance d'BP = 4 h'BS h'UT fc/c", $h' = h - h_E$, "For UMi hE = 1.0m", c = 3.0 × 10⁸ m/s; for UMa $h_E$ is random. [Established] [38.901 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.901/38901-j50.zip)
- **UMi-Street Canyon LOS beyond the breakpoint (Table 7.4.1-1, re-read 21 Nov 2026 from the saved text):** $PL_{UMi-LOS} = PL_1$ for 10 m ≤ $d_{2D}$ ≤ $d'_{BP}$ and $PL_2$ for $d'_{BP}$ ≤ $d_{2D}$ ≤ 5 km, with "PL2=32.4+40log10(d3D)+20log10(fc) -9.5log10((dBP')2+(hBS-hUT)2)", σ_SF = 4, 1.5 m ≤ h_UT ≤ 22.5 m, h_BS = 10 m; NLOS is max(PL_UMi-LOS, PL_UMi-NLOS') for 10 m ≤ d_2D ≤ 5 km. [Established]

*Same source as R-283; the row labels (mean, then standard deviation, of log10(DS/1 s)) were confirmed on 21 Nov 2026 from the table layout and Note 4 ("mean (μlgX = mean{log10(X)}) and standard deviation (σlgX = std{log10(X)})").*

Notes: "NOTE 1: DS = rms delay spread"; "the delay distribution is modelled by an exponential distribution"; "For UMa and frequencies below 6 GHz, use fc = 6"; "For UMi and frequencies below 2 GHz, use fc = 2"; values marked Note 8 "were updated as part of Release 19 Study on channel modelling enhancements for 7-24 GHz". **Part-2:** InH LOS $-0.01\log_{10}(1 + f_c) - 7.692$ / 0.18, InH NLOS $-0.28\log_{10}(1 + f_c) - 7.173$ / $0.10\log_{10}(1 + f_c) + 0.055$ ("For InH and frequencies below 6 GHz, use fc = 6"); RMa means −7.49 / −7.43 / −7.47. The pre-Release-19 values were not checked. [Established]

## R-285 · 3GPP NR numerology and frame structure (TS 38.211)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP archive and the ETSI PDF of TS 38.211 V19.5.0; re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **TS 38.211 V19.5.0 (2026-09), Table 4.2-1:** μ = 0…6 with $\Delta f = 2^\mu \cdot 15$ kHz (15 to 960 kHz), normal cyclic prefix for all and extended for μ = 2. **Clause 4.3.1:** "frames with Tf = … = 10 ms duration, each consisting of ten subframes of Tsf = … = 1 ms duration". **Table 4.3.2-1:** 14 OFDM symbols per slot for every μ (normal CP), 10·2^μ slots per frame, 2^μ slots per subframe; Table 4.3.2-2: 12 symbols per slot for extended CP (μ = 2). **Clause 4.4.4.1:** "A resource block is defined as N_sc^RB = 12 consecutive subcarriers in the frequency domain." Derived (ours): slot durations 1, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125 ms … for μ = 0, 1, 2, 3. [Established] [38.211 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.211/38211-j50.zip)

## R-286 · NR OFDM symbol and cyclic-prefix timing (TS 38.211)

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 from the ETSI PDF of TS 38.211 V19.5.0 saved by a research subagent (its equations extract as text).*

- **TS 38.211 V19.5.0 (2026-09), clause 4.1:** "time units Tc = 1/(Δfmax · Nf) where Δfmax = 480·10³ Hz and Nf = 4096. The constant κ = Ts/Tc = 64 where Ts = 1/(Δfref · Nf,ref), Δfref = 15·10³ Hz and Nf,ref = 2048." **Clause 5.3.1:** useful symbol $N_u^\mu = 2048\kappa \cdot 2^{-\mu}$; cyclic prefix $N_{CP,l}^\mu = 512\kappa \cdot 2^{-\mu}$ (extended), $144\kappa \cdot 2^{-\mu} + 16\kappa$ (normal, $l = 0$ or $l = 7 \cdot 2^\mu$) and $144\kappa \cdot 2^{-\mu}$ (normal, other $l$), in units of $T_c$. **Clause 6.3.1.4 "Transform precoding"**: applied to the uplink when enabled (per TS 38.214), with a single layer. Derived (ours): $T = 1/\Delta f$; normal CP = 144/2048 of $T$ (4.69 μs at 15 kHz, 2.34 μs at 30 kHz); each subframe sums to exactly 1 ms. [Established] [ETSI TS 138 211](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/138200_138299/138211/19.05.00_60/ts_138211v190500p.pdf)

## R-288 · Wi-Fi 6/7 OFDM features and 802.11be/bn status

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. IEEE 802.11 standards are paywalled and the IEEE SA page for 802.11be returned HTTP 403; the facts below come from IEEE 802.11 task-group pages (primary for status), Wi-Fi Alliance pages and Keysight documentation (secondary for technical features).*

- **IEEE 802.11 TGbe update page:** 802.11be defines EHT PHY and MAC layers "capable of supporting a maximum throughput of at least 30 Gbps"; D7.0 "received final approval from the IEEE SA Standards Board in September 2024". [Established] [TGbe](https://www.ieee802.org/11/Reports/tgbe_update.htm)
- **IEEE 802.11 TGbn update page (Nov 2026):** 802.11bn targets 1–7.25 GHz with "Ultra High Reliability"; D2.0 letter ballot done July 2026; D3.0 January 2027; RevCom/SASB approval projected May 2028. [Established] [TGbn](https://www.ieee802.org/11/Reports/tgbn_update.htm)
- **Wi-Fi Alliance (MAC/PHY page; Wi-Fi CERTIFIED 7 highlights, Jan 2024):** Wi-Fi 6 uses OFDMA, 160 MHz channels and 1024-QAM; Wi-Fi 7 adds 320 MHz channels (6 GHz band), 4K-QAM ("20% higher transmission rates than 1024 QAM"), multi-link operation and multiple resource units per station. [Established, secondary] [WFA](https://www.wi-fi.org/wi-fi-macphy)
- **Keysight N7617 "802.11ax Introduction" (undated):** resource units; downlink and uplink MU-MIMO on RUs of at least 106 tones with up to eight users; "Up to 1024QAM"; "OFDM with reduced sub-carrier spacing (4x symbol length of 802.11ac)". The final 802.11be spatial-stream count was not verified from a primary source. [Established, secondary] Keysight N7617 "802.11be Introduction" (undated) lists "Binary convolutional coding / Low Density Parity Check (LDPC)" and 20–320 MHz bandwidths. [Established, secondary] [Keysight](https://helpfiles.keysight.com/csg/n7617/Content/Main/802.11be%20Introduction.htm) [Keysight](https://helpfiles.keysight.com/csg/n7617/Content/Main/802.11ax%20Introduction.htm)

## R-289 · NR UE capabilities: maximum data rate and MIMO layers (TS 38.306)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP archive (38306-j40.zip); the data-rate clause and layer requirement re-checked against the saved text on 21 Nov 2026.*

- **TS 38.306 V19.4.0 (2026-09), clause 4.1.2 "Supported max data rate for DL/UL":** approximate rate $= 10^{-6}\sum_j v_{Layers}^{(j)}Q_m^{(j)}f^{(j)}R_{max}\frac{N_{PRB}^{BW(j),\mu}\cdot12}{T_s^\mu}(1 - OH^{(j)})$ Mb/s, with "Rmax = 948/1024", $T_s^\mu$ "the average OFDM symbol duration in a subframe … normal cyclic prefix is assumed" ($10^{-3}/(14 \cdot 2^\mu)$), scaling factor $f$ ∈ {1, 0.8, 0.75, 0.4}, and overhead "0.14, for frequency range FR1 for DL; 0.18, for frequency range FR2 for DL; 0.08, for frequency range FR1 for UL; 0.10, for frequency range FR2 for UL". **maxNumberMIMO-LayersPDSCH:** "it is mandatory with capability signalling to support at least 4 MIMO layers in the bands where 4Rx is specified as mandatory for the given UE and at least 2 MIMO layers in FR2". Derived (ours): 100 MHz FR1, 30 kHz, 273 RBs, 4 layers of 256-QAM → 2337 Mb/s. [Established] [38.306 archive](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.306/38306-j40.zip)

## R-291 · 3GPP release status (Releases 15–20)

*Read on 20 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the 3GPP website; re-checked against the saved pages on 21 Nov 2026.*

## R-292 · Ethernet framing (RFC 894; IEEE 802.3 interpretation)

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 with curl from rfc-editor.org and ieee802.org; the IEEE 802.3 standard itself is paywalled and was not read.*

- **RFC 894 (1984), "A Standard for the Transmission of IP Datagrams over Ethernet Networks":** Ethernet with "48-bit addresses"; "The minimum length of the data field of a packet sent over an Ethernet is 46 octets", padded with zeros if necessary; "the maximum length of an IP datagram sent over an Ethernet is 1500 octets" (the preceding sentence in the RFC says "minimum" where "maximum" is meant). [Established] [RFC 894](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc894.txt)
- **IEEE 802.3 interpretation request 1-11/09:** the committee's response states "The 96 bit value defined in sub clause 4.4.2 therefore applies at MAC transmit"; the request's own accounting uses "1518:data+8:preamble+12:IPG" bytes per maximum-size frame. Derived (ours): 1518 = 14-byte header + 1500 payload + 4-byte FCS; minimum frame 64 bytes = 14 + 46 + 4. [Established] [IEEE 802.3 interp 1-11/09](https://www.ieee802.org/3/interp/interp-1-1109.pdf)

## R-293 · IP, UDP, TCP, ARP, addressing and TLS (IETF RFCs)

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 with curl from rfc-editor.org.*

- **RFC 791 (IPv4):** Total Length allows datagrams "up to 65,535 octets"; all hosts must accept datagrams "of up to 576 octets"; Time to Live bounds a datagram's lifetime. **RFC 768 (UDP):** "Length is the length in octets of this user datagram including this header" (8-byte header: ports, length, checksum). **RFC 9293 (TCP, 2022):** 4-bit Data Offset (header length in 32-bit words; 20-byte minimum header), 16-bit Window field, "the three-way (or three message) handshake". **RFC 826:** Address Resolution Protocol. **RFC 1918:** private ranges "10.0.0.0 - 10.255.255.255 (10/8 prefix)", "172.16.0.0 - 172.31.255.255 (172.16/12 prefix)", "192.168.0.0 - 192.168.255.255 (192.168/16 prefix)". **RFC 4632:** CIDR. **RFC 8200 (IPv6):** 128-bit source and destination addresses, Hop Limit and Flow Label fields (40-byte fixed header), and every link "must be configured to have an MTU of at least 1280 octets". **RFC 2453 (RIP v2):** a metric of 16 "is normally referred to as 'infinity'". **RFC 8446 (TLS 1.3):** full handshake in one round trip, with an optional 0-RTT mode and its replay caveats. [Established] [RFC index](https://www.rfc-editor.org/)

## R-294 · TCP congestion control, timers and window scaling

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 with curl from rfc-editor.org.*

- **RFC 5681:** initial window "IW = 2 * SMSS bytes and MUST NOT be more than 2 segments" if SMSS > 2190 bytes, 3 segments if 1095 < SMSS ≤ 2190, 4 if SMSS ≤ 1095; slow start and congestion avoidance with "cwnd += SMSS*SMSS/cwnd" per ACK; slow-start threshold. **RFC 6298:** first measurement "SRTT <- R, RTTVAR <- R/2"; then "RTTVAR <- (1 - beta) * RTTVAR + beta * |SRTT - R'|", "SRTT <- (1 - alpha) * SRTT + alpha * R'", "RTO <- SRTT + max (G, K*RTTVAR)" with K = 4, "alpha=1/8 and beta=1/4"; "(2.4) Whenever RTO is computed, if it is less than 1 second, then the RTO SHOULD be rounded up to 1 second." **RFC 7323:** Window Scale option shift.cnt; "the shift count MUST be limited to 14 (which allows windows of 2^30 = 1 GiB)". [Established] [RFC 5681](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5681.txt), [RFC 6298](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6298.txt), [RFC 7323](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7323.txt)

## R-296 · TCP congestion control in practice: CUBIC, the initial window and timeout behaviour

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 from the RFC Editor's plain-text copies (rfc-editor.org), primary.*

- **RFC 9438, "CUBIC for Fast and Long-Distance Networks" (Standards Track, August 2023):** "CUBIC has been adopted as the default TCP congestion control algorithm by the Linux, Windows, and Apple stacks"; it "sets the multiplicative window decrease factor to 0.7, whereas Reno" uses 0.5, and "The parameter β_cubic SHOULD be set to 0.7". [Established]
- **RFC 6928, "Increasing TCP's Initial Window" (Experimental, April 2013):** raises the initial window "to 10 segments (maximum 14600 B)", specified as "min (10*MSS, max (2*MSS, 14600))". [Established]
- **RFC 5681 §3.1:** after a retransmission timeout, "ssthresh = max (FlightSize / 2, 2*SMSS)" and cwnd is set to "no more than the loss window, LW, which equals 1 full-sized segment". [Established]
- **RFC 6298:** "(5.5) The host MUST set RTO <- RTO * 2 ("back off the timer")"; "TCP MUST use Karn's algorithm [KP87] for taking RTT samples. That is, RTT samples MUST NOT be made using segments that were retransmitted". [Established]

## R-297 · Arm Cortex-M processor comparison (architecture, pipeline, CoreMark/MHz)

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (direct fetch of Arm's documentation service and product pages).*

- **Arm, "Cortex-M Processor Comparison Table", document 102787, version 3.0 (metadata 2024-12-11)** ([PDF](https://documentation-service.arm.com/static/655e085f2c8b3557fee7048f)): architecture M0+ "Armv6-M"; M3, M4, M7 "Armv7-M" (Arm's M4 and M7 product pages and data sheets say "Armv7E-M", that is Armv7-M with the DSP extension); M23 "Armv8-M Baseline"; M33 "Armv8-M Mainline"; M52, M55, M85 "Armv8.1-M Mainline". Rows "DMIPS/MHz*" and "CoreMark®/MHz*": M0+ 0.99 / 2.46; M3 1.24 / 3.45; M4 1.26 / 3.54; M7 2.31 / 5.29; M23 1.03 / 2.64; M33 1.54 / 4.10; M52 1.60 / 4.30; M55 1.69 / 4.40; M85 3.13 / 6.28 (Arm Compiler 6.17; 6.18 for the M85). Options: FPU M4 and M33 "SP (option)", M7 "SP, DP (option)"; Helium M52 "Single-beat (option)", M55 and M85 "Dual-beat (option)"; TrustZone M23/M33/M52/M55 "Yes (option)", M85 "Yes"; caches and TCMs only on M7, M52, M55, M85. [Established]
- **Arm product pages (support.arm.com/compute-ip/cortex-x), pipelines:** M0+ "2-stage"; M3 "Three-stage"; M4 "3-stage"; M23 "Two-stage"; M33 "Three-stage"; M7 "6-stage superscalar and branch prediction"; M52 "4-stage pipeline"; M55 "4-stage (for main integer pipeline)"; M85 "7-stage (for main integer pipeline)". [Established]
- **Older data sheets (© 2020) give lower figures:** M4 "CoreMark/MHz 3.42", M7 "5.01", M33 "4.02" (compiler improvements). [Established]

## R-298 · Cortex-M interrupt latency

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from Arm technical reference manuals.*

- **Cortex-M3 TRM DDI0337H and Cortex-M4 TRM DDI0439B:** "There is a maximum of a 12 cycle latency from asserting the interrupt to execution of the first instruction of the ISR when the memory being accessed has no wait states"; "Tail chaining requires 6 cycles when using zero wait state memory." [Established] ([M4 TRM](https://documentation-service.arm.com/static/5f19da2a20b7cf4bc524d99a))
- **Cortex-M0+ TRM DDI0484C:** "The worst case interrupt latency, for the highest priority active interrupt in a zero wait-state system not using jitter suppression, is 15 cycles." [Established]
- **Cortex-M23 TRM DDI0550D:** "If the Security Extension is not implemented or when a short stack is used, the interrupt latency is 15 cycles"; with a full stack (Non-secure exception interrupting Secure code) "the interrupt latency is 25 cycles." [Established]
- **Cortex-M7:** "Typically 12, worst case 14" (Arm white paper "ARM Cortex-M for Beginners", J. Yiu, March 2017, read from a copy hosted on a university course site: secondary hosting). The M52, M55 and M85 TRMs state no latency figure. [Established, secondary hosting for the M7]

- **Exception return (Cortex-M3 TRM 100165_0201_02_en r2p1 §3.9.2, same text in DDI 0337H; Cortex-M4 TRM DDI 0439B/C §3.9.1):** "Returns from interrupts similarly take twelve cycles where the instruction being returned to is fetched in parallel to the stack pop" (documentation-service.arm.com, read 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent; the older M3 TRMs DDI 0337E/G give no return figure). [Established]

## R-304 · NVIC sizes, priority bits and vendor interrupt controllers

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from Arm architecture manuals and data sheets, ST's CMSIS device header and Espressif's data sheet.*

- **Armv7-M ARM DDI0403E.e:** "The Armv7-M NVIC architecture supports up to 496 interrupts"; priority fields are "an IMPLEMENTATION DEFINED power of two in the range 8 to 256… All priority value fields are 8-bits" (that is, 3 to 8 implemented bits). **Armv8-M ARM DDI0553B.z:** "The Nested Vectored Interrupt Controller (NVIC) can support up to 496 external interrupts". **Armv6-M ARM DDI0419E:** "supports up to 32 discrete external interrupts" and "2-bit priority fields, providing four priority levels". [Established]
- **Core maxima:** Cortex-M4 data sheet "External interrupts, configurable from 1 to 240" and "Bits of priority, configurable from 3 to 8"; Cortex-M33 "configurable from 1 to 480"; Arm's comparison table gives M23 240 and M33/M52/M55/M85 480; the M23 TRM "Up to 240 external interrupt inputs, each with four levels of priority". [Established]
- **STM32F4:** ST's CMSIS device header (github.com/STMicroelectronics/cmsis-device-f4, stm32f407xx.h): "#define __NVIC_PRIO_BITS 4U /*!< STM32F4XX uses 4 Bits for the Priority Levels */" (the programming manual itself could not be fetched). [Established]
- **Espressif ESP32-C3 data sheet v2.5 (2026-11-11):** "Up to 32 vectored interrupts at seven priority levels". [Established]

## R-305 · DMA controllers in microcontrollers

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from vendor data sheets, Arm's TRM and ST's HAL source (ST's reference manual could not be fetched).*

- **Microchip SAM D5x/E5x DMAC** (data sheet DS60001507N): "Up to 32channels"; "4 configurable priority levels for each channel"; "Multi-buffer or circular buffer modes by linking multiple descriptors"; "From 1 to 256KB data transfer in a single block transfer"; "The DMAC has four bus interfaces". Bus matrix: "Symmetric crossbar bus switch implementation"; "Allows concurrent accesses from different Hosts to different Clients"; "32-bit data bus"; hosts include the CM4, cache controller, DMAC read and write, ICM and DSU; clients include flash, SRAM ports and AHB-APB bridges; a four-level SRAM quality-of-service setting. [Established]
- **Arm PrimeCell µDMA controller PL230, TRM DDI0417A** ([PDF](https://documentation-service.arm.com/static/5f74b4911b758617cd95b9e6)): "up to 32 channels"; "performs all DMA transfers using the SINGLE AHB-Lite burst type"; "transfers in a single DMA cycle can be programmed from 1 to 1024"; ping-pong and scatter-gather cycle types. [Established]
- **STM32F4 DMA** (ST's HAL source, stm32f4xx_hal_dma_ex.c, on ST's GitHub): two controllers with eight streams each (DMA1_Stream0 to DMA2_Stream7); memory burst options "DMA_MBURST_INC4/INC8/INC16"; FIFO thresholds from 1/4 to full; "When Multi (Double) Buffer mode is enabled the, transfer is circular by default." [Established, from source code rather than the reference manual]

## R-306 · I²C, I3C, SPI, UART and GPIO electrical facts

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent with curl and pdftotext. nxp.com (UM10204 itself), st.com and docs.nordicsemi.com blocked automated access; TI, Microchip, MIPI and kernel documents were used instead, and the source type is marked on each line.*

- **I²C specification:** UM10204 "I2C-bus specification and user manual Rev. 7.0 — 1 October 2021" (search snippet of nxp.com, secondary); the Linux kernel documentation: "version 7 as of this writing" and "In earlier I2C specifications, controller was named "master" and target was named "slave". These terms have been obsoleted with v7 of the specification" ([kernel docs](https://docs.kernel.org/i2c/summary.html), secondary). [Established]
- **I²C speed modes:** Sm 100 kbit/s, Fm 400 kbit/s, Fm+ 1 Mbit/s, Hs 3.4 Mbit/s, UFm 5 Mbit/s; "Ultra-Fast mode (UFm)… is write-only" (TI SBAA565, Nov 2022, Table 1-1, secondary). **Rise time and bus capacitance:** t_r max 1000 / 300 / 120 ns and "Cb Capacitive load for each bus line 400 400 550 pF" for Sm / Fm / Fm+; "Low-level output voltage (at 3 mA current sink, VCC > 2 V) 0.4 0.4 0.4 V"; t_r = 0.8473 R_p C_b (TI SLVA689A, Table 2-1, secondary). **Minimum sink current:** 3 mA, 3 mA, 20 mA at 100 kHz, 400 kHz, 1 MHz (Microchip TB2020, secondary). **Input levels:** "input high voltage level as 70% of VDD… input low voltage level as 30% of VDD" (Microchip TB2020, secondary). **Reserved 7-bit addresses** (TI SBAA565 Table 5-1, secondary): 000 0000 general call (R/W = 0) or START byte (R/W = 1), 000 0001 C-Bus, 000 0010 other bus format, 000 0011 future, 000 01XX Hs-mode controller code, 111 11XX device ID, 111 10XX 10-bit addressing. [Established]
- **MIPI I3C** ([MIPI page](https://www.mipi.org/specifications/i3c-sensor-specification), primary): "MIPI I3C® v1.2 (February 2025)… MIPI I3C Basic™ v1.2 (April 2025)"; "I3C v1.2 does not introduce any new features". MIPI I3C v1.2 FAQ (public edition, Q13.3, primary): "The base raw bitrate for SDR Mode is 12.5 Mbps, with 11 Mbps real data rate at 12.5 MHz clock frequency"; "The maximum raw bitrate is 33.3 Mbps at 12.5 MHz, with real data rate of 30 Mbps" (HDR, one lane); Q1.4: "higher throughput for a given frequency, In-Band Interrupts (from Target to Controller), Dynamic Addressing, advanced power management, and Hot-Join"; Q15.1: "most Legacy I²C Target devices can be operated on an I3C Bus, provided they have a 50 ns spike (glitch) filter and do not attempt to stall the clock"; Q2.4: SPI "has many different implementations because there is no clearly defined standard." [Established]
- **SPI modes** (Microchip ATmega328P data sheet DS40002061B, Table 19-2, primary): mode 0 CPOL 0 CPHA 0 sample on rising edge; mode 1 CPOL 0 CPHA 1 falling; mode 2 CPOL 1 CPHA 0 falling; mode 3 CPOL 1 CPHA 1 rising. **MSPM0G350x SPI clock** (TI SLASEX6C rev. Oct 2025, §7.21, primary): up to 32 MHz at 2.7–3.6 V with high-speed I/O; "Two SPIs, one SPI supports up to 32Mbits/s". [Established]
- **UART receiver tolerance** (ATmega328P data sheet §20.8.3, Tables 20-2 and 20-3, primary): for 8 data-plus-parity bits, 16× sampling, Rslow 95.36 % and Rfast 104.58 %, recommended maximum receiver error ±2.0 %; 8× sampling, Rslow 96.00 %, Rfast 103.90 %, ±1.5 %. (The table's "−4.54" slow-side total is a typo for −4.64, per the data sheet's own equation.) TI SLAU846F §25.2.3.2 (primary): "Oversampling by 16 to increases the tolerance of the receiver to clock deviations. The maximum speed is limited to UARTclk/16." [Established]
- **GPIO, TI MSPM0G350x** (SLASEX6C rev. Oct 2025, primary): VIH ≥ 0.7·VDD, VIL ≤ 0.3·VDD, hysteresis 0.1·VDD; pull-ups and pull-downs 40 kΩ typical; standard-drive pins at VDD ≥ 2.7 V and 6 mA: VOL ≤ 0.4 V, VOH ≥ VDD − 0.4 V (−40 to 25 °C); absolute maximum 6 mA per standard pin, 20 mA per high-drive pin, 80 mA total into VDD (Tj ≤ 130 °C), injection current ±2 mA; "Two 5V-tolerant open drain IOs", other pins limited to VDD + 0.3 V; ESD HBM ±2000 V, CDM ±500 V. [Established]

- **I²C fast-mode drive (UM10204 Rev. 7.0, 1 Oct 2021, Table 10, read 21 Nov 2026 from a verbatim copy of NXP's PDF hosted by Pololu, secondary hosting):** I_OL at V_OL = 0.4 V is 3 / 3 / 20 mA (Sm / Fm / Fm+), and at V_OL = 0.6 V 6 mA (Fm only); footnote [4]: "In order to drive full bus load at 400 kHz, 6 mA IOL is required at 0.6 V VOL. Parts not meeting this specification can still function, but not at 400 kHz and 400 pF." Table 11: Fm t_r max 300 ns, C_b max 400 pF. [Established]

- **TI MSPM0G350x ADC** (data sheet SLASEX6C rev. Oct 2025, §7.12, primary): 12-bit SAR at up to 4 Msps (5.3 Msps in 8-bit mode); ENOB 11.1 typical, 10.9 minimum, with an external reference at f_in = 10 kHz; 12.5 typical with hardware averaging of 16 samples and a 2-bit shift; 10.8 typical with the internal 2.5 V reference; SNR 68 dB typical (external reference); INL ±2 LSB, DNL ±1 LSB with no missing codes; sample-and-hold capacitance 3.3 pF, input (switch) resistance 0.5 kΩ; minimum sampling time 62.5 ns in 12-bit mode with R_S = 50 Ω and C_pext = 10 pF. Feature list: "14-bit effective resolution at 250ksps with hardware averaging". Technical reference manual SLAU846F §18.1 (primary): "Integrated hardware oversampling for averaging up to 128 samples". [Established]
- **TI MSPM0G350x DAC** (SLASEX6C §7.17, primary): 12-bit, up to 1 Msps; ENOB 11.5 typical, SINAD 71.1 dB, SNR 80.9 dB (4 kHz tone at 1 Msps with an output low-pass filter); DNL ±1 LSB, INL ±4 LSB; full-scale settling 0.8 µs typical, 1 µs maximum; slew rate 5.5 V/µs. [Established]
- **Raspberry Pi RP2040 ADC** ([RP2040 data sheet](https://datasheets.raspberrypi.com/rp2040/rp2040-datasheet.pdf), primary): "12-bit with 8.7 ENOB", 500 ksps; measured at 250 ksps with a 997 Hz tone, ENOB 8.6 / 8.7 / 8.8 (min / typ / max), SNR 61.5 dB and SINAD 54.0 dB typical; input impedance at least 100 kΩ. Erratum RP2040-E11: "The ENOB for the ADC has been reduced from 9-bits (simulated) to 8.7-bits (measured)", caused by DNL spikes at codes 512, 1536, 2560 and 3584. [Established]
- **Raspberry Pi RP2350 ADC** ([RP2350 data sheet](https://datasheets.raspberrypi.com/rp2350/rp2350-datasheet.pdf), primary): 500 kS/s; the feature list says "12-bit with 9.2 ENOB" while the ADC electrical table gives ENOB 9 minimum and 9.5 typical, and the INL/DNL section says "Details to follow": an inconsistency within the document, reported as found. [Established, internally inconsistent source]

## R-308 · RTOS releases, priority inheritance and the Mars Pathfinder account

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent with curl (primary sources unless marked).*

- **FreeRTOS kernel:** current release V11.3.3, "Changes between FreeRTOS V11.3.2 and FreeRTOS V11.3.3 released October 29, 2026" ([History.txt at tag V11.3.3](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/FreeRTOS/FreeRTOS-Kernel/V11.3.3/History.txt)); earlier V11.3.2 (16 Oct 2026), V11.3.1 (Aug 2026), V11.3.0 (Mar 2026). [Established]
- **FreeRTOS LTS:** current 202604 LTS with kernel 11.3.0; "FreeRTOS long term support (LTS) releases receive security and critical bug fixes (should any be necessary) for at least two years following their release" ([LTS libraries page](https://www.freertos.org/Documentation/03-Libraries/01-Library-overview/03-LTS-libraries/01-LTS-libraries), "Last Updated: April 29, 2026"). The GitHub FreeRTOS-LTS README still describes 202406 LTS (stale). [Established]
- **FreeRTOS mutexes** ([mutex documentation](https://www.freertos.org/Documentation/02-Kernel/02-Kernel-features/02-Queues-mutexes-and-semaphores/04-Mutexes)): "if a high priority task blocks while attempting to obtain a mutex (token) that is currently held by a lower priority task, then the priority of the task holding the token is temporarily raised to that of the blocking task"; "Priority inheritance does not cure priority inversion! It just minimises its effect in some situations"; "A task remains at its highest inherited priority until it has released all the mutexes it holds." [Established]
- **Zephyr:** "Zephyr 4.5.0 2026-10-28 2027-10-28 Latest stable … Zephyr 3.7.0 (LTS3) 2024-07-26 2029-07-27 Long Term Support" ([release index](https://docs.zephyrproject.org/latest/releases/index.html)); 4.4.0 was 2026-04-14; the next release, 4.6 (April 2027), is designated LTS4. [Established]
- **Mars Pathfinder priority inversion:** Glenn Reeves (JPL flight-software lead), 15 Dec 1997, archived in RISKS 19.54 ([RISKS 19.54](https://catless.ncl.ac.uk/Risks/19/54), secondary archive of the original email): "The higher priority bc_dist task was blocked by the much lower priority ASI/MET task that was holding a shared resource"; the fix was to "change the creation flags for the semaphore so as to enable the priority inheritance"; and, correcting an earlier account, "No, we did not use the vxWorks shell to change the software" (the patch was uploaded as a validated difference image). The earlier account is Mike Jones's RISKS 19.49 post of 7 Dec 1997 relaying a keynote by Wind River's David Wilner (landing 4 July 1997, VxWorks). [Established]

## R-309 · Bootloaders, update schemes and update standards

*Read on 21 Nov 2026 by a research subagent with curl, git ls-remote and pdftotext (primary sources unless marked).*

- **MCUboot:** "MCUboot is a secure bootloader for 32-bits microcontrollers" ([docs.mcuboot.com](https://docs.mcuboot.com/)). Current release v2.5.0 (git tag dated 2026-10-22); v2.4.0 was 2026-04-23 ([repository tags](https://github.com/mcu-tools/mcuboot), [release notes](https://docs.mcuboot.com/release-notes.html)); the documentation home page still says "This document is for release v2.4.0". Upgrade modes ([design document](https://docs.mcuboot.com/design.html)): overwrite-only, swap-using-scratch, swap-using-move, swap-using-offset, direct-xip and ram-load; "the swap-using-scratch algorithm may be removed in the coming future"; swap-using-offset "is preferred over swap-using-move except when building for existing products already using the latter"; "If the device crashes immediately upon booting a new (bad) image, MCUboot will revert to the old (working) image at the next device reset". [Established]
- **IETF SUIT:** RFC 9019, "A Firmware Update Architecture for Internet of Things", April 2021, Informational ([rfc9019.txt](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9019.txt)); RFC 9124, "A Manifest Information Model for Firmware Updates in Internet of Things (IoT) Devices", January 2022, Informational ([rfc9124.txt](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9124.txt)); the manifest format is draft-ietf-suit-manifest-38 (2026-11-04), intended Proposed Standard, "IESG state RFC Ed Queue … RFC Editor status Awaiting Editor Assignment" ([datatracker](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-suit-manifest/)), so not yet an RFC as of 21 Nov 2026. [Established]
- **Arm PSA Certified Firmware Update API:** version 1.0.1, "Document number: IHI 0093 Release Quality: Final … Date of Issue: 23/9/2025"; earlier 1.0.0 (Aug 2023), 1.0 Beta (Oct 2022), 0.7 Beta (Jan 2021); the project "is maintained by GlobalPlatform" ([psa-api fwu](https://arm-software.github.io/psa-api/fwu/)). [Established]
- **ROM bootloaders:** TI MSPM0G350x bootstrap loader "enables configuration of the device as well as programming of the device memory through a UART or I2C serial interface", password-protected (256-bit), can be disabled, entered by an invoke pin, automatically on a blank device or by a software reset command (SLASEX6C §8.33); Raspberry Pi RP2040 16 kB boot ROM with a "USB MSC class-compliant bootloader with UF2 support for downloading code/data to FLASH or RAM" and a "USB PICOBOOT bootloader interface for advanced management" (RP2040 data sheet §2.8). [Established]
