- **Noise, kickback, offset references:** P. Nuzzo et al., *IEEE TCAS-I* 55(6), pp. 1441–1454, July 2008, DOI 10.1109/TCSI.2008.917991 (time-domain noise analysis; no abstract numbers). P. M. Figueiredo, J. C. Vital, *IEEE TCAS-II* 53(7), pp. 541–545, July 2006, DOI 10.1109/TCSII.2006.875308 (kickback defined; no abstract numbers). B. Wicht et al., *IEEE JSSC* 39(7), pp. 1148–1158, July 2004 (offset σ from 19 to 8.5 mV by input common mode). **UNVERIFIED:** measured kickback magnitudes.
- **Bootstrapped switch:** B. Razavi, "The bootstrapped switch," *IEEE SSC Magazine* 7(3), pp. 12–15, Summer 2015, DOI 10.1109/MSSC.2015.2449714 (precharged-capacitor idea traced to Brandt, Ferguson, Rebeschini, 1997; modifications by Dessouky & Kaiser, Electron. Lett., Jan. 1999, and Abo & Gray, 1999; culminating in Dessouky & Kaiser 2001; complementary switches "inadequate for linearities above approximately 6 b"). A. M. Abo, P. R. Gray, *IEEE JSSC* 34(5), pp. 599–606, May 1999, DOI 10.1109/4.760369 (bootstrapping "that does not subject the devices to large terminal voltages"; 58.5 dB SNDR, 36 mW; the JSSC abstract's DNL value is garbled in the accessible copy, UNVERIFIED). M. Dessouky, A. Kaiser, *IEEE JSSC* 36(3), pp. 349–355, Mar. 2001, DOI 10.1109/4.910473 (locally bootstrapped switch avoiding gate-dielectric overstress; 1 V, 88 dB DR). The prehistory (Brandt 1997, 1966 patent) is known only through Razavi (Medium).
- **Metastability:** C. L. Portmann, T. H. Y. Meng, *IEEE JSSC* 31(8), pp. 1132–1140, Aug. 1996, DOI 10.1109/4.508260 (7 b, 80 MHz flash; measured < 10⁻¹² errors/cycle with error reduction vs an estimated 10⁻⁴ without). A. Waters, J. Muhlestein, U.-K. Moon, *IEEE TCAS-I* 63(11), pp. 1898–1909, Nov. 2016, DOI 10.1109/TCSI.2016.2594256 (uniform-input assumption overestimates SMR by up to 18 dB). **UNVERIFIED:** a 2018–2026 SAR abstract stating an absolute measured metastability error rate.

## R-332 · Sampling jitter: formula, surveys and published values (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from TI documents (ti.com), OpenAlex/Crossref abstracts and the Murmann survey files (raw.githubusercontent.com).*

- **Formula:** T. Neu, "Clock jitter analyzed in the time domain, Part 1," TI *Analog Applications Journal* SLYT379, 3Q 2010: "SNR_Jitter[dBc] = −20 × log(2π × f_IN × t_Jitter)"; total jitter is the root-sum-square of clock-input and ADC aperture jitter; aperture jitter rises as the clock slew rate falls; example 400 fs at 100 MHz → 72 dBc. Re-read 24 Nov 2026 (pp. 5–9): "A 14-bit pipeline converter typically has a thermal noise of ~70 to 74 dB"; "A 16-bit ADC, for example, has a thermal noise floor of ~77 to 80 dB" (the 16-bit sentence does not say pipeline); 73 dB thermal plus 400 fs at 100 MHz gives 69.5 dBc. Part 2 (SLYT389, 4Q 2010) gives measured baselines instead: ADS54RF63 64.4 dBFS, ADS5483 79.1 dBFS. National Semiconductor AN-775 (TI SNOA223), 1992: aperture jitter specified as rms; peak-slope rule f_max = 1/(2π t_aj 2^n).
- **Walden:** R. H. Walden, *IEEE JSAC* 17(4), pp. 539–550, Apr. 1999, DOI 10.1109/49.761034: from about 2 MS/s to 4 GS/s resolution falls about 1 bit per doubling of sample rate, attributed to aperture jitter; comparator ambiguity limits multi-GS/s rates. Body text UNVERIFIED.
- **Murmann survey:** "ADC Performance Survey 1997-2026," github.com/bmurmann/ADC-survey, latest revision 20260801 (VLSI 2026 update); jitter lines for 1 ps and 0.1 ps (64.04 dB at 1 GHz for 0.1 ps). ISSCC 2022 short course: "High-speed ADCs and DACs: Tens of femtoseconds timing jitter"; τ ≈ 3 ps "in leading-edge technology". Implied jitter bounds of about 51–55 fs from survey data are the subagent's derivation (Medium), not stated values.
- **Stated jitter values in abstracts:** M. Straayer et al., *ISSCC* 2016, paper 27.5, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2016.7418108 ("SNR is dominated by aperture jitter of 50fs" at 3.8 GHz); S. Devarajan et al., *ISSCC* 2009, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2009.4977320 (65 fs); A. M. A. Ali et al., *ISSCC* 2010, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2010.5433923 (60 fs).

- **ADC12DJ5200RF** (SLVSEN9H, rev. Aug 2026): 12 b, 10.4 GS/s single-channel; −3 dB bandwidth 8 GHz; aperture jitter 50 fs (dither off) / 60 fs (dither on) at the minimum coarse delay setting; SNR 49.6 dBFS at 7997 MHz (−1 dBFS, dual-channel); code error rate 10⁻¹⁸ errors/sample, attributed to comparator metastability ("sparkle codes"), excluding JESD204C interface BER; vendor claim "multiple orders of magnitude better" than pipelined flash or SAR [Contested].

- **ADC32RF55** (SBAS500B, rev. Aug 2023): 14 b, up to 3 GS/s; aperture jitter 50 fs; full-power bandwidth 2.75 GHz; CER 10⁻¹⁵.
- **ADC12DJ3200** (SLVSD97A, rev. Apr 2020): 50 fs aperture jitter; 8 GHz bandwidth; CER 10⁻¹⁸.
- **LF398** (SNOSBI3C, rev. Oct 2018): classic sample-and-hold; hold step 0.5 mV at 0.01 µF; "Hold step, acquisition time, and droop rate are the major trade-offs in the selection of a hold capacitor value."

## R-335 · Converter test standards and TI measurement notes (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent. standards.ieee.org was unreachable and the standards are paywalled, so editions come from Crossref/OpenAlex records and citing articles, and definitions from committee members' papers and vendor notes.*

- **IEEE Std 1241-2023** (ADC terminology and test methods), DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2023.10269815, ISBN 9781504496902; current edition (a 2026 I2MTC abstract cites "IEEE Standard 1241-2023"). **IEEE Std 1658-2023** (DACs), DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2023.10285677, confirmed by P. Crovetti et al., *IEEE Instrum. Meas. Mag.* 28(4), pp. 7–12, June 2025, DOI 10.1109/MIM.2025.11021346. **UNVERIFIED:** the 2023 normative wording (ENOB form, terminal-based vs independently based INL, number of THD harmonics); the 1241-2000 edition's ENOB = log2(FSR/(√12 e_rms)) is described by Blair & Linnenbrink (2002).
- **TI SLAA510** (C. Pearson, "High-Speed, Analog-to-Digital Converter Basics," Jan. 2011): ENOB = (SINAD − 1.76)/6.02; ideal SNR = 6.02n + 1.76; SNR, SFDR, THD, SINAD in dBc or dBFS; THD over the first five harmonics (some data sheets use nine). **TI SLYT231**: dBc = dBFS + A_IN. **TI SBAU128C** (*ADCPro User's Guide*, rev. C, Mar. 2012): coherent sampling needs an integer number of cycles relatively prime to the record length, an odd count sufficing for a power-of-two record. **TI SLVAG04** (R. Reeder, "Coherently Sampling in High-Speed Data-Converter Testing," Feb. 2025): worked example 5.2 GS/s, 65,536 points, bin 12,743 → 1011.1022949 MHz.

## R-336 · ADC figure-of-merit frontier and converter data sheets (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent from the Murmann survey spreadsheet (revision 20260801, 763 usable rows, via raw.githubusercontent.com), the ISSCC 2022 short course PDF, OpenAlex abstracts and TI data sheets. The frontier values are the subagent's extraction from the survey (recomputed FoMs match the spreadsheet); abstract confirmation is noted.*

- **Definitions (survey Readme sheet):** FoM_W = P/(f_snyq 2^ENOB) with ENOB = (SNDR − 1.76)/6.02; FoM_S = SNDR + 10 log10(f_snyq/2/P), f_snyq = f_s/OSR. No DR-based column; Murmann's 2022 slide 62 gives the DR form too.
- **Best FoM_W:** 0.354 fJ/conv-step, H. S. Bindra, A.-J. Annema, S. M. Louwsma, B. Nauta, "A 0.2–8 MS/s 10b flexible SAR ADC achieving 0.35–2.5 fJ/conv-step and using self-quenched dynamic bias comparator," VLSI Symp. 2019, pp. C74–C75, DOI 10.23919/VLSIC.2019.8778093 (65 nm, 200 kS/s; abstract confirms 0.35 fJ). Unchanged since 2019.
- **Best FoM_S (SNDR):** 189.17 dB, H. Zhao et al., "A fully dynamic noise-shaping SAR ADC achieving 120dB SNDR and 189dB FoMs in 1kHz BW," ISSCC 2025, paper 18.1, pp. 306–308, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49661.2025.10904778 (0.18 µm, OSR 1024, 139 µW; title numbers). Best Nyquist-rate: 186.83 dB, S.-E. Hsieh, C.-C. Hsieh, ISSCC 2018, pp. 240–242, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2018.8310273.
- **High speed:** ≥100 MS/s: 181.49 dB, J.-C. Wang, T.-H. Kuo, ISSCC 2022, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC42614.2022.9731546 (0.82 mW, 130 MS/s, 14b pipelined-SAR; title numbers). ≥1 GS/s: 173.3 dB, Y. Cao et al., ISSCC 2025, paper 24.2, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49661.2025.10904766 (abstract: 28 nm, 68.2 dB SNDR, 15.3 mW, "Schreier FoM of 173.3dB"); best FoM_W ≥1 GS/s 5.06 fJ (S.-E. Hsieh, T.-C. Wu, C.-C. Hou, ISSCC 2023, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC42615.2023.10067258, abstract). ≥10 GS/s: 159.3 dB, Y. Cao et al., ISSCC 2024, paper 22.1, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49657.2024.10454350 (abstract: 12 GS/s, 54.1 dB SNDR, 179.8 mW, 36.2 fJ/conv-step).
- **DR-based outlier (subagent's computation):** Wu et al., ISSCC 2020, paper 9.1, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC19947.2020.9062990, 202.96 dB on DR vs 164.86 dB on SNDR; the abstract attributes the 140 dB DR to extending the input range and disconnecting inactive DAC cells (signal-dependent noise floor).
- **8kT·SNR bound:** Murmann ISSCC 2022 short course slide 66: line "P/f_s = 8kT×SNR", "corresponds to rail-to-rail class-B circuits [Vittoz, 1990]"; slide 64: envelope roll-off ≈ −10 dB/decade above about 10⁷ S/s. E. A. Vittoz, *Proc. ISCAS* 1990, pp. 1372–1375, DOI 10.1109/ISCAS.1990.112386 (body not read). B. Murmann, "Energy limits in A/D converters," FTFC 2013, DOI 10.1109/FTFC.2013.6577781: best converters "about two orders of magnitude above the classical 8kT·SNR thermal limit" (as of 2013).
- **Data sheets:** ADC12DJ5200RF (SLVSEN9H, rev. Aug 2026): 12-bit no missing codes; DNL +0.14/−0.13 LSB, INL +2.0/−2.4 LSB (typ.); at 2397 MHz, −1 dBFS, dual channel: SNR 54.7 dBFS, SINAD 53.9 dBFS, ENOB 8.7 (front page 8.6), SFDR 65 dBFS; at 7997 MHz: SINAD 46.2 dBFS, ENOB 7.4, SFDR 50 dBFS. ADS8861 (rev. A, Dec 2013): 16 b, 1 MSPS SAR; INL ±1 LSB (endpoint), DNL ±1 LSB, 16-bit no missing codes; SNR 96.5 dB, SINAD 96.4 dB, THD −112 dB (front page −115 dB); 5.5 mW at 1 MSPS. ADS127L11 (rev. C, Sep 2022): 24-bit ΔΣ; INL 0.9 ppm (best-fit); DR 111.5 dB, SNR 110 dB, THD −120 dB; 18.6 mW; quotes "effective resolution" without the √12 factor.

## R-337 · SAR ADC literature: charge redistribution, switching energy, asynchronous SAR (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: IEEE metadata from Crossref, abstracts from OpenAlex (IEEE Xplore not used), Razavi 2015 from the author-hosted PDF.*

- **J. L. McCreary and P. R. Gray**, "All-MOS charge redistribution analog-to-digital conversion techniques—Part I," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. SC-10, no. 6, pp. 371–379, Dec. 1975, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1975.1050629: binary-weighted capacitor array performs successive approximation with inherent sample/hold; 10 bits, 23 µs conversion. Razavi (2015, R-338) notes CMOS SARs from the 1970s "lay virtually dormant until the 2000s, when their potential for low power consumption was recognized."
- **C.-C. Liu, S.-J. Chang, G.-Y. Huang, Y.-Z. Lin**, "A 10-bit 50-MS/s SAR ADC with a monotonic capacitor switching procedure," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 45, no. 4, pp. 731–740, Apr. 2010, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2010.2042254 (abstract): average switching energy and total capacitance "reduced by about 81% and 50%" against the conventional procedure; input common mode "gradually converges to ground"; 0.13 µm, 1.2 V, 50 MS/s, SNDR 57.0 dB, 0.826 mW, 29 fJ/conversion-step. The closed forms 1363.3 and 255.5 $C_uV_{REF}^2$ (10 bits) are this curriculum's derivation and simulation (verify_20_07.py), which reproduce the 81% and 50%; they were not read in the paper's full text.
- **B. P. Ginsburg and A. P. Chandrakasan**, ISCAS 2005, pp. 184–187, DOI 10.1109/ISCAS.2005.1464555 (abstract): splitting the MSB capacitor reduces average switching energy by 37% against conventional switching (10-bit array, HSPICE). JSSC vol. 42, no. 4, pp. 739–747, Apr. 2007, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2007.892169: 500 MS/s 5-bit ADC of 6 interleaved SARs, 6 mW, chosen "due to its simplicity versus flash and its amenability to scaled technologies versus pipelined, which relies on operational amplifiers."
- **V. Hariprasath, J. Guerber, S.-H. Lee, U.-K. Moon**, *Electronics Letters*, vol. 46, no. 9, pp. 620–621, Apr. 2010, DOI 10.1049/el.2010.0706 (abstract): merged-capacitor switching uses 93.4% less switching energy than "the conventional architecture". Percentages in these papers are each against its own baseline (UNVERIFIED that the baselines agree).
- **S.-W. M. Chen and R. W. Brodersen**, "A 6-bit 600-MS/s 5.3-mW asynchronous ADC in 0.13-µm CMOS," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 41, no. 12, pp. 2669–2680, Dec. 2006, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2006.884231 (abstract): two interleaved asynchronous SARs; "avoids clocks at higher than the sample rate"; nonbinary search with digital radix calibration; SNDR 34 dB, 5.3 mW.
- **Calibration of SAR mismatch (abstracts):** H.-S. Lee, D. A. Hodges, P. R. Gray, *IEEE JSSC* SC-19(6), pp. 813–819, Dec. 1984, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1984.1052231 (self-calibrating 15-bit); M. Ding et al., *IEEE JSSC* 52(2), pp. 423–432, Feb. 2017, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2016.2609849 (13 b 6.4 MS/s, background mismatch and offset calibration, 46 µW, 64.1 dB SNDR); H. Li et al., ISSCC 2018, pp. 242–244, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2018.8310274: "One-time factory calibration suffers from aging, temperature sensitivity, and package and PCB stress while foreground calibration precludes continuous ADC operation."

## R-338 · Pipeline and time-interleaving literature (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (with R-337); Razavi 2013 and 2015 read in full from author-hosted PDFs at seas.ucla.edu/brweb.*

- **S. H. Lewis and P. R. Gray**, *IEEE JSSC* SC-22(6), pp. 954–961, Dec. 1987, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1987.1052843: pipelined 5 MS/s 9-bit ADC "with digital correction", 3 µm CMOS, 180 mW.
- **S. H. Lewis, H. S. Fetterman, G. F. Gross, R. Ramachandran, T. R. Viswanathan**, "A 10-b 20-Msample/s analog-to-digital converter," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 27, no. 3, pp. 351–358, Mar. 1992, DOI 10.1109/4.121557: nine-stage pipeline, 0.9 µm CMOS, 60 dB SNDR at 5 MHz, 240 mW.
- **B. Razavi**, "A tale of two ADCs: Pipelined versus SAR," *IEEE Solid-State Circuits Magazine*, vol. 7, no. 3, pp. 38–46, Summer 2015, DOI 10.1109/MSSC.2015.2442372: decision levels at ±V_REF/4, cited to Lewis et al. 1992; "can tolerate a comparator offset as high as ±VREF/4"; "half a bit of 'redundancy'"; capacitor mismatch and finite gain errors "can be removed by digital calibration"; op-amp limits "created a migration to SAR architectures". Razavi does not call Lewis 1992 the first 1.5-bit design (UNVERIFIED which design was first).
- **B. Murmann and B. E. Boser**, "A 12-bit 75-MS/s pipelined ADC using open-loop residue amplification," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 38, no. 12, pp. 2040–2050, Dec. 2003, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2003.819167: digital background calibration replaces precision amplifiers; more than 60% residue-amplifier power savings; 290 mW at 3 V.
- **B. Hershberg, S. Weaver, K. Sobue, S. Takeuchi, K. Hamashita, U.-K. Moon**, "Ring amplifiers for switched capacitor circuits," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 47, no. 12, pp. 2928–2942, Dec. 2012, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2012.2217865: 15-bit pipeline, 76.8 dB SNDR at 20 MS/s, 5.1 mW.
- **C. C. Lee and M. P. Flynn**, "A SAR-assisted two-stage pipeline ADC," *IEEE JSSC* 46(4), pp. 859–869, Apr. 2011, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2011.2108133: 12 b 50 MS/s, 10.4 ENOB at Nyquist, without calibration.
- **W. C. Black and D. A. Hodges**, "Time interleaved converter arrays," *IEEE JSSC* SC-15(6), pp. 1022–1029, Dec. 1980, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1980.1051512: four-way array of 7-bit converters.
- **B. Razavi**, "Design considerations for interleaved ADCs," *IEEE JSSC*, vol. 48, no. 8, pp. 1806–1817, Aug. 2013, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2013.2258814: two channels; offset mismatch gives a tone at f_S/2; gain mismatch α gives SNR = 4/α² (eq. 20); timing mismatch gives an image at f_S/2 − f_in with SNR = 1/(π²ΔT²f_in²) (eq. 22), which "overestimates the corruption by considering only the highest input frequency"; background timing calibration reduces the image to −75 dB for inputs above 500 MHz. Reproduced numerically (verify_20_07.py).
- **Other analyses and calibrations (abstracts):** N. Kurosawa et al., *IEEE TCAS-I* 48(3), pp. 261–271, Mar. 2001, DOI 10.1109/81.915383 (offset effect independent of gain and timing effects); M. El-Chammas and B. Murmann, *IEEE JSSC* 46(4), pp. 838–847, Apr. 2011, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2011.2108125 (12 GS/s 5-bit TI flash, correlation-based background skew calibration, 12 dB improvement); S. M. Jamal et al., *IEEE JSSC* 37(12), pp. 1618–1627, Dec. 2002, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2002.804327 (correlation-based gain and sample-time calibration).

Nani et al.**, "A 5nm 60GS/s 7b 64-way time interleaved partial loop unrolled SAR ADC achieving 34dB SNDR up to 32GHz," VLSI Symp. 2024, DOI 10.1109/VLSITechnologyandCir46783.2024.10631522 (abstract): comparator offsets calibrated in background "by detecting patterns in the SAR output decisions"; 34.3 dB SNDR up to 32 GHz; 109.3 mW from 0.9 V.

Nguyen et al.**, "A 200GS/s 8b 20fJ/c-s receiver with >60GHz AFE bandwidth for 800Gb/s optical coherent communications in 5nm FinFET," ISSCC 2024, pp. 344–346, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49657.2024.10454385 (abstract): integrated in an 800G coherent DSP. - **Zhang et al.**, "A 72GS/s 9b time-interleaved pipeline-SAR ADC achieving 55.3/49.3dB SFDR at 20GHz/Nyquist inputs in 16nm FinFET," ISSCC 2025, pp. 436–438, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49661.2025.10904672 (abstract): 1.125 GS/s unit ADCs; 37.0 dB SNDR at Nyquist; 393.6 mW; "53.7fJ/conv.-step FoMs" (a Walden figure mislabelled); "Relying solely on timing skew calibration … is not sufficient since it only provides first-order correction for bandwidth mismatch."

Core architecture not stated (UNVERIFIED).
- **ADC12DJ5200RF** (SLVSEN9H, rev. Aug 2026): "a total of six sub-ADCs, each referred to as a bank, with two banks forming an ADC core"; in background calibration "the third ADC core, ADC C, is swapped in periodically for ADC A and ADC B"; "small brief glitches may still occur on the converter data as the cores are swapped"; whole-chip power 4.01 W (single channel, foreground calibration), 4.90 W (background calibration), 5.03 W (dual channel, background); f_S/2 − f_IN spur −68 dBFS typical (−56 dBFS max) at 2397 MHz; "SNR results exclude DC, HD2 to HD9 and interleaving spurs; SINAD, ENOB, and SFDR results exclude DC and fixed-frequency interleaving spurs"; dual-channel SINAD 53.9 dBFS at 2397 MHz.

## R-341 · Delta-sigma classics: origins, MASH, continuous-time loops, decimation, fractional-N (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: IEEE metadata from Crossref, abstracts from OpenAlex; full texts of Matsuya et al. (1987, course-hosted copy) and Razavi (2016, author-hosted) read.*

- **H. Inose, Y. Yasuda, J. Murakami**, *IRE Trans. Space Electronics and Telemetry*, vol. SET-8, no. 3, pp. 204–209, Sept. 1962, DOI 10.1109/IRET-SET.1962.5008839: integration added to a delta modulator, "named delta-sigma modulation after its modulation mechanism". Inose and Yasuda, *Proc. IEEE* 51(11), pp. 1524–1535, 1963, DOI 10.1109/PROC.1963.2622: "periodical noise which are inherent to the proposed system". Razavi (2016) cites an earlier patent (Brahm, 1961), not read.
- **J. C. Candy**, *IEEE Trans. Commun.* 33(3), pp. 249–258, 1985, DOI 10.1109/TCOM.1985.1096276: double integration; "when three or more loops are used the circuit can latch into undesirable overloading modes"; "a simple linear theory gives a good description … even when the quantization has only two levels".
- **Y. Matsuya et al.** (NTT), *IEEE JSSC* SC-22(6), pp. 921–929, Dec. 1987, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1987.1052839: 16-bit, 24 kHz, triple-integration MASH ("Hereafter, this technique will be referred to as MASH"), 91 dB S/(N+THD).
- **B. E. Boser and B. A. Wooley**, *IEEE JSSC* 23(6), pp. 1298–1308, 1988, DOI 10.1109/4.90025: second-order modulator design from behavioral models of circuit impairments; 89 dB dynamic range, 12 mW, 3 µm CMOS.
- **K. C.-H. Chao, S. Nadeem, W. L. Lee, C. G. Sodini**, *IEEE Trans. Circuits Syst.* 37(3), pp. 309–318, Mar. 1990, DOI 10.1109/31.52724: higher-order interpolative modulator topology; higher order randomizes the noise, "avoiding the need for dithering" (authors' claim). W. L. Lee's 1987 MIT thesis was not found (UNVERIFIED title wording).
- **J. A. Cherry and W. M. Snelgrove**, *IEEE TCAS-II* 46(4), pp. 376–389, Apr. 1999, DOI 10.1109/82.755409 (excess loop delay breaks the CT-to-DT equivalence; remedies) and 46(6), pp. 661–676, June 1999, DOI 10.1109/82.769775 (CT loops suffer "more severely from time jitter in the quantizer clock than discrete-time designs").
- **E. B. Hogenauer**, *IEEE Trans. ASSP* 29(2), pp. 155–162, Apr. 1981, DOI 10.1109/TASSP.1981.1163535: CIC filters "require no multipliers and use limited storage".
- **T. A. D. Riley, M. A. Copeland, T. A. Kwasniewski**, *IEEE JSSC* 28(5), pp. 553–559, May 1993, DOI 10.1109/4.229400: ΔΣ modulation in fractional-N synthesis gives "beneficial shaping of the phase noise (jitter) introduced by fractional-N division".
- **B. Razavi**, "The delta-sigma modulator [A circuit for all seasons]," *IEEE SSC Magazine* 8(2), pp. 10–15, Spring 2016, DOI 10.1109/MSSC.2016.2543061: ΔΣ is "An efficient solution for resolutions above approximately 12 b"; kT/C capacitors smaller "by a factor of M"; the second-order loop "the most commonly used DSM for moderate-performance applications"; CT advantages (no front-end sampler, low kickback, inherent anti-aliasing) and drawbacks (jitter, op-amp bandwidth, metastability, resistor noise); tones and dithering.

## R-342 · Delta-sigma teaching sources: the noise-shaping law, Lee's rule, conventions, jitter model (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (with R-341) from author and course PDFs.*

- **R. Schreier, ECE1371 slides** ("Higher-Order ΔΣ Modulators and the ΔΣ Toolbox", University of Toronto): in-band noise ≈ π^{2N}σ_e²/((2N+1)OSR^{2N+1}), "(6N+3) dB/octave"; slide 20 "The Lee Criterion for Stability in a 1-bit Modulator: ‖H‖∞ ≤ 2 [Wai Lee, 1987]", not necessary ("MOD2 is stable … but ‖H‖∞ = 4") nor sufficient ("‖H‖∞ ≤ 1.5 often works"); "Mod1+Mod2" slides: simulated SQNR at OSR 128 of 55 dB (MOD1, full scale) and 86 dB (MOD2, 75% of full scale). **"The One-Page Story"** (Analog Devices, 1998–2008): "Lee's Rule (with margin): ‖H‖∞ < 1.5 ⇒ probably stable"; toolbox default `synthesizeNTF(… Hinf=1.5 …)`. R. Schreier, *IEEE TCAS-II* 40(8), pp. 461–466, 1993, DOI 10.1109/82.242348: "none of the existing criteria for stability are adequate for design".
- **D. Johns and K. Martin, "Oversampling Converters" slides** (University of Toronto, from *Analog Integrated Circuit Design*, 1997): SNR_max = 6.02N + 1.76 − 5.17 + 30 log OSR (first order) and − 12.9 + 50 log OSR (second order), with full scale a sine of peak 2^N(Δ/2); sinc^{L+1} decimation: "Use L + 1 cascade to roll off quantization noise faster than it rises". The 6.02 dB difference from the ±1-level convention for 1-bit quantizers is this curriculum's derivation.
- **R. Saad, S. Hoyos, S. Palermo**, InTech chapter, 2012, DOI 10.5772/46453: NRZ jitter error ε(n) = (y(n) − y(n−1))Δt(n)/T_s; "in NRZ waveforms the clock-jitter will be effective only during the clock edges at which data is changing".
- **Books:** *Understanding Delta-Sigma Data Converters*, 2nd ed., Wiley/IEEE Press, 2017, DOI 10.1002/9781119258308 (Crossref author order Schreier, Pavan, Temes; often cited with Pavan first). Delta Sigma Toolbox: BSD-style licence (via the python-deltasigma LICENSE); its File Exchange version (2020.1.0) is from a search summary only (UNVERIFIED); python-deltasigma 0.2.2 (Aug 2015) uses `collections.Iterable` and `np.float`, removed in Python 3.10 and NumPy 1.24 (static inspection, not executed).

## R-346 · DAC mismatch yield and dynamic element matching (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent (with R-345); Cong's dissertation and the Heydenreich et al. arXiv paper read in full.*

- **Yield rule σ(I)/I ≤ 1/(2C√2^N)**, attributed to Van den Bosch, Steyaert and Sansen (ISCAS 2000, DOI 10.1109/ISCAS.2000.858699; *AICSP* 29(3), 2001, DOI 10.1023/A:1011261330190): the printed form was not read (UNVERIFIED as printed). Cong (PhD, Iowa State, 2002) describes it as "based on a nonstandard INL definition and a questionable statistical assumption, where each current output is compared to the ideal value without correcting for the gain error"; Cong's Monte Carlo gives 0.22% for 14 bits at 99% yield.
- **G. I. Radulov, M. Heydenreich, R. van der Hofstad, J. A. Hegt, A. H. M. van Roermund**, *IEEE TCAS-II* 54(2), pp. 146–150, Feb. 2007, DOI 10.1109/TCSII.2006.886900; arXiv:math/0606584: "For the thermometer case, the functional is the maximal absolute value of the Brownian bridge." The curriculum's Kolmogorov-quantile rule (λ = 1.818 at 99.73%) and its Monte Carlo agree.
- **Pelgrom et al.**, *IEEE JSSC* 24(5), pp. 1433–1439, Oct. 1989, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1989.572629.
- **R. J. van de Plassche**, "Dynamic element matching for high-accuracy monolithic D/A converters," *IEEE JSSC* 11(6), pp. 795–800, Dec. 1976, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1976.1050820 (Crossref and OpenAlex metadata; ISSCC 1976 Digest pp. 148–149, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.1976.1155525): the method "combines passive division using matched elements with a time division concept, needs no trimming, and is insensitive to element aging" (abstract). That it introduced DEM is secondary (Jiang, Olleta, Chen and Geiger, *IEEE TIM* 56(5), 2007, DOI 10.1109/TIM.2007.903621: "The DEM technique, first introduced by Van De Plassche in 1976"), read through Semantic Scholar's citation contexts, 24 Nov 2026; the paper itself not read.
- **L. R. Carley**, *IEEE JSSC* 24(2), pp. 267–273, Apr. 1989, DOI 10.1109/4.18585: multibit noise-shaping coder with "a novel form of dynamic element matching". **R. T. Baird and T. S. Fiez**, *IEEE TCAS-II* 42(12), pp. 753–762, 1995, DOI 10.1109/82.476173: data weighted averaging shapes DAC errors by "first-order noise shaping".

## R-349 · PLL theory literature: sampled-loop limits, phase-noise conventions, oscillator noise, ADPLL (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: IEEE metadata from Crossref, abstracts from OpenAlex; Banerjee's book, Hanumolu et al., Hajimiri and Lee with corrections, Lee and Hajimiri's tutorial, McNeill and the NIST paper read in full. Gardner's and Leeson's original texts not read.*

- **F. M. Gardner**, "Charge-pump phase-lock loops," *IEEE Trans. Commun.* 28(11), pp. 1849–1858, Nov. 1980, DOI 10.1109/TCOM.1980.1094619. Stability limit as quoted by D. Fischette's ISSCC 2004 tutorial: "ωn² < ωref²/(π·(Rlpf·C1·ωref + π))"; equivalent bandwidth form ωc < ωREF/[π(1 + πωz/ωREF)] (TAMU course notes); →f_ref/π for ωz ≪ ωref (our algebra). Gardner's own wording UNVERIFIED.
- **P. K. Hanumolu et al.**, *IEEE TCAS-I* 51(9), pp. 1665–1674, Sep. 2004, DOI 10.1109/TCSI.2004.834516: "IC designers have been faithful in keeping the loop bandwidth of the PLL to about 1/10 of the PFD update frequency (or lower)"; third-order loop unstable at a reference-to-bandwidth ratio "around 3"; at 70° PM stable at 3.7, unstable at 3.3.
- **D. Banerjee**, *PLL Performance, Simulation, and Design*, 5th ed., TI SNAA106C, May 2017: bandwidth "no more than about one-tenth of the phase detector frequency"; instability "when the loop bandwidth approaches 28% of the phase detector frequency"; integrated phase noise A = 2∫L(f)df; table of peak-to-peak/rms ratios 4.65 (N = 50), 6.58 (10³), 9.78 (10⁶), 12.22 (10⁹), which equal 2Q⁻¹(1/2N) (our check); phase noise varies "board to board and part to part… in the order of a few dB". Errors found: worked example 0.1 µs/72 printed as 1.339 ns (correct 1.389 ns); "99%" within 3σ (correct 99.73%); eq. 38.19 C1 ratio inverted in the extracted text.
- **D. B. Leeson**, *Proc. IEEE* 54(2), pp. 329–330, 1966, DOI 10.1109/PROC.1966.4682 (original not read; factor-of-2 placement varies between texts). **A. Hajimiri and T. H. Lee**, *IEEE JSSC* 33(2), pp. 179–194, Feb. 1998, DOI 10.1109/4.658619, corrections *ibid.* 33(6), p. 928, DOI 10.1109/4.678662 (factor 8 → 4 in (19), 4 → 2 in (21)). **T. H. Lee and A. Hajimiri**, *IEEE JSSC* 35(3), pp. 326–336, Mar. 2000, DOI 10.1109/4.826814: Leeson's F "is an empirical fitting parameter"; the 1/f³ corner "is not necessarily the same as the 1/f device/circuit noise corner".
- **B. Razavi**, *IEEE JSSC* 31(3), pp. 331–343, Mar. 1996, DOI 10.1109/4.494195. **J. A. McNeill**, *IEEE JSSC* 32(6), pp. 870–879, June 1997, DOI 10.1109/4.585289: free-running jitter "increases as the square root of the number of delays"; in a PLL the asymptotes "intersect at the loop bandwidth time constant".
- **T. A. D. Riley, M. A. Copeland and T. A. Kwasniewski**, *IEEE JSSC* 28(5), pp. 553–559, May 1993, DOI 10.1109/4.229400.
- **R. B. Staszewski et al.**, *IEEE JSSC* 39(12), pp. 2278–2291, Dec. 2004, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2004.836345 (130 nm Bluetooth; VCO, PFD and charge pump "replaced with a digitally controlled oscillator and a time-to-digital converter"); *IEEE JSSC* 40(12), pp. 2469–2482, Dec. 2005, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2005.857417 ("the first all-digital PLL and polar transmitter for mobile phones", 90 nm). TDC in-band floor (2π)²/12·(Δt f0)²/f_ref is our derivation; attribution to a specific paper UNVERIFIED.
- **X. Gao, E. A. M. Klumperink, M. Bohsali and B. Nauta**, *IEEE JSSC* 44(12), pp. 3253–3263, Dec. 2009, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2009.2032723: PD/CP noise "not multiplied by N²"; 2.2 GHz, 0.18 µm, 4.2 mA at 1.8 V, 0.15 ps rms (10 kHz–40 MHz); FoM −247.7 dB (our arithmetic).
- **F. L. Walls et al.** (NIST), *Proc. 42nd Frequency Control Symposium*, 1988: S_φ(f) = L(ν0−f) + L(ν0+f) ≈ 2L(f); "L(f) and dBc/Hz are single sideband measures of phase noise".

FoM = 10 log[(σ_t/1 s)²(P/1 mW)]; printed equation UNVERIFIED (full text 403), definition confirmed by numeric consistency with several abstracts.

Razavi**, "Jitter-power trade-offs in PLLs," *IEEE TCAS-I* 68(4), pp. 1381–1387, Apr. 2021, DOI 10.1109/TCSI.2021.3057580: "to halve σj, PVCO must quadruple"; with reference noise "PVCO must rise by a factor of 16 for every halving of σj"; oscillator FoM ≈ 186 dB for Q = 10 at 300 K (eq. 7, FOM = 10 log[Q²/(10³πkT(1+γ))], for a cross-coupled LC VCO with γ = 1, single-ended swing ≈ V_DD/2, P_VCO = I_SS·V_DD, tail and flicker noise neglected; 185.8 dB our check), class-C example 196 dB "with Q ≈ 16" (Mazzanti and Andreani, JSSC 2008, as cited by Razavi); re-read 24 Nov 2026 from the author copy; 112 Gbaud TX PLL "less than roughly 100 fs_rms"; 256QAM near 30 GHz needs integrated phase jitter of −45 dB, "30 fs at 30 GHz" (29.8 fs, our check); crystal oscillators −153 to −158 dBc/Hz at 100 kHz for "less than 0.2 mW", a 100 MHz oscillator at −178 dBc/Hz "draws 1.8 W"; clock generation "may consume a greater power than the converter itself".
- **W. Bae**, *IEEE Access* 10, pp. 80680–80694, 2022, DOI 10.1109/ACCESS.2022.3195687 (abstract only): FoM "assumptions have started breaking".
- **P. Davami and T. Jang**, "Phase-Locked Loops (PLL) Performance Survey," ETH Zürich, MIT license, gitlab.ethz.ch/eecis/pll_survey (last commit 2026-05-08): JSSC Jan 2020–May 2026, ISSCC 2020–2026, other conferences 2020–2025; 228 entries; 23 FoMs differ from 20 log J + 10 log P by more than 0.6 dB (our check).
- **Records (abstracts):** Y. Zhao and B. Razavi, VLSI 2021, DOI 10.23919/VLSICircuits52068.2021.9492419 (19 GHz, 20.3 fs 10 kHz–100 MHz, 12 mW, 28 nm; −263.1 dB ours) and JSSC 58(6) 2023, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2022.3225105 (20.9 fs "random jitter" 10 kHz–40 MHz); D. Sun et al., ISSCC 2025 19.11, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49661.2025.10904662 (13 GHz CP-PLL, 15.8 fs, −98.5 dBc reference spur; band UNVERIFIED); ISSCC 2026 12.4, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49663.2026.11409271 (fractional-N, 21.6 fs, −260.7 dB, worst-case spur −67.4 dBc, 14 GHz, 28 nm, 18.1 mW); C.-C. Gallucci et al., JSSC 60(12) 2025, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3618958 (DPLL, 45.9 fs, −257 dB, 4.75 GHz); D. - **TI LMX2594** (SNAS696C, rev. Apr. 2019): 10 MHz–15 GHz; "–110 dBc/Hz phase noise at 100-kHz offset with 15-GHz carrier"; "45-fs rms jitter at 7.5 GHz (100 Hz to 100 MHz)"; "Figure of merit: –236 dBc/Hz"; "Normalized 1/f noise: –129 dBc/Hz"; f_PD 400 MHz integer, 300 MHz fractional; ΔΣ up to 4th order. Model: "PLL_flat = PLL_FOM + 20 × log(Fvco/Fpd) + 10 × log(Fpd / 1Hz)" (−114.4 dBc/Hz at 12 GHz, f_PD 100 MHz: our arithmetic). I_CC 340 mA typical at 3.3 V.

- **TI AWR2944** (SWRS273E, rev. Feb. 2026): "Ultra-accurate chirp engine based on fractional-N PLL"; −96 dBc/Hz at 1 MHz (76–77 GHz), −95 dBc/Hz (76–81 GHz); ramp rate to 250 MHz/µs. Feature list prints "12dBm" for the 12 dB noise figure. **AWR1843** (SWRS222D): −95/−93 dBc/Hz at 1 MHz; 100 MHz/µs.

## R-353 · Mismatch literature: Pelgrom, Lakshmikumar, Kinget (Course 20)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: Crossref metadata and OpenAlex abstracts; Pelgrom 1989 and Pelgrom–Tuinhout–Vertregt 1998 read in full (scanned copies); Kinget and Steyaert's 1997 chapter read in full (author-hosted). Kinget 2005 full text not read (abstract only).*

- **M. J. M. Pelgrom, A. C. J. Duinmaijer and A. P. G. Welbers**, *IEEE JSSC* 24(5), pp. 1433–1439, Oct. 1989, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1989.572629: σ²(ΔP) = A_P²/(WL) + S_P²D² for the difference of two devices; Table I (2.5 µm, 50 nm oxide): A_VT0 = 30 (n) / 35 (p) mV·µm, A_β = 2.3 / 3.2 %·µm, S_VT0 = 4 µV/µm; cross-coupled quad cancels "the linear components in the gradient"; "the estimation of the standard deviation of an ideal normal distribution by means of 100 samples leads to a relative error larger than 10 percent once out of six times" (χ² check: 15.9%).
- **Lakshmikumar, Hadaway and Copeland**, *IEEE JSSC* 21(6), pp. 1057–1066, Dec. 1986, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1986.1052648: 3 µm CMOS data; mismatch models "relate the mismatch to the device dimensions"; DAC yield "greater than 97%".
- **P. R. Kinget**, *IEEE JSSC* 40(6), pp. 1212–1224, June 2005, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2005.848021 (abstract): "accuracy requirements impose a minimal device area"; "a fixed bandwidth-accuracy-power tradeoff which is set by technology constants"; "For MOS circuits the power constraints due to matching are several orders of magnitude higher than for thermal noise." Exact equations of the 2005 paper UNVERIFIED (paywalled). **Kinget and Steyaert**, CICC 1996, DOI 10.1109/CICC.1996.510569 ("two orders higher"), and the 1997 Springer chapter, DOI 10.1007/978-1-4757-2580-3_2: P = 24·C_ox·A_VT0²·f·DR² (3σ, class-B) against 8kT·f·DR²; its Table 3.4 energies do not reproduce from its stated constants.
- **M. J. M. Pelgrom, H. P. Tuinhout and M. Vertregt**, IEDM 1998, pp. 915–918, DOI 10.1109/IEDM.1998.746503: A_VT "reduces with decreasing gate oxide thickness"; its main contribution is "the uncertainty in the number of active doping atoms in the depletion layer"; "Optimum circuit performance can only be achieved when the matched circuit elements are designed identical with respect to geometry, rotation, biasing and temperature"; describes the Monte Carlo flow for global and mismatch variation.

Grenoble 2014 (HAL tel-01305050): pair constants 2.4 mV·µm (28 nm bulk gate-first) and 1.9 mV·µm (20 nm gate-last); 28/14 nm FD-SOI ≈ 1.3/1.4 mV·µm. **Paul et al.**, IEDM 2013, DOI 10.1109/IEDM.2013.6724625: 14 nm SOI FinFET 1.4 (n) / 0.7 (p) mV·µm (definition not stated in the abstract). **Giles et al.**, VLSI 2015, DOI 10.1109/VLSIT.2015.7223657: Intel 14 nm single-fin σV_t 19 mV (N), 24 mV (P), "normally distributed out to +/-5σ".

## R-361 · S-parameter theory, stability criteria, VNA calibration and de-embedding references (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: Crossref and OpenAlex metadata and abstracts; archival PDFs of HP AN 95-1 (xdevs.com) and AN 1287-3 (qsl.net). Keysight's current editions are behind a registration gate and were not read.*

- **Kurokawa**, "Power Waves and the Scattering Matrix," *IEEE Trans. MTT* 13(2):194–202, Mar. 1965, DOI 10.1109/TMTT.1965.1125964. Abstract: the squared magnitudes of the waves are "directly related to the exchangeable power of a source and the reflected power"; gives "the condition under which an amplifier can be matched simultaneously at both input and output ports as well as the condition for the network to be unconditionally stable" in scattering-matrix terms. Reference impedance may be complex. [Established] (Kurokawa's affiliation not checked.)
- **Marks and Williams**, *J. Res. NIST* 97(5):533–562, 1992, DOI 10.6028/jres.097.024 (end page from secondary listing): distinguishes "traveling waves, constructed with respect to a well-defined characteristic impedance, from a set of pseudo-waves, defined with respect to an arbitrary reference impedance." Williams, *IEEE Microwave Mag.* 14(7):38–45, 2013, DOI 10.1109/MMM.2013.2279494. [Established]
- **HP AN 95-1** (1997 edition): "based on an article written for the February 1967 issue of the Hewlett-Packard Journal" (R. W. Anderson); cites Kurokawa's generalized scattering parameters. [Established]
- **Rollett**, *IRE Trans. Circuit Theory* 9(1):29–32, 1962, DOI 10.1109/TCT.1962.1086854: "k = 1 defines the boundary between unconditional and conditional stability ... the basic invariant stability factor" (defined with immittance parameters; correction 1963, DOI 10.1109/TCT.1963.1082074). **Woods**, *IEEE Trans. Circuits Syst.* 23(2):73–81, 1976, DOI 10.1109/TCS.1976.1084179 (S-parameter reappraisal). **Edwards and Sinsky**, *IEEE Trans. MTT* 40(12):2303–2311, 1992, DOI 10.1109/22.179894: "mu >1 alone is necessary and sufficient for a circuit to be unconditionally stable." [Established]
- **Engen and Hoer** (TRL), *IEEE Trans. MTT* 27(12):987–993, 1979, DOI 10.1109/TMTT.1979.1129778: "the requirement for either a known short, or a 'calibration circuit' eliminated." **Marks** (multiline TRL), *IEEE Trans. MTT* 39(7):1205–1215, 1991, DOI 10.1109/22.85388: "multiple, redundant transmission line standards ... improvements in both accuracy and bandwidth." [Established]
- **HP AN 1287-3**, "Applying Error Correction to Network Analyzer Measurements," 5965-7709E, 1997: six systematic error types per direction, "a total of twelve error terms"; TRL "primarily used in noncoaxial environments, such as testing waveguide, using test fixtures, or making on-wafer measurements with probes"; in coax "SOLT is usually the preferred calibration technique"; "An adapter with a VSWR of 1.5:1 for example, will reduce the effective directivity of a test coupler to about 14 dB, even if the coupler itself has infinite directivity." [Established]
- **Koolen, Geelen and Versleijen**, Proc. BCTM 1991, pp. 188–191, DOI 10.1109/BIPOL.1991.160985 (open–short on-wafer de-embedding); Tiemeijer and Havens, *IEEE TED* 50(3):822–829, 2003, DOI 10.1109/TED.2003.811396. Rytting, "ARFTG 50 year network analyzer history," 71st ARFTG, 2008, DOI 10.1109/ARFTG.2008.4633319. [Established]

## R-365 · On-chip spiral inductors: Q, shields, modeling and formulas (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: OpenAlex abstracts and Crossref metadata.*

- **Burghartz, Edelstein, Soyuer, Ainspan and Jenkins**, *IEEE JSSC* 33(12):2028–2034, Dec. 1998, DOI 10.1109/4.735544: "Integrated spiral inductors with inductances of 0.5-100 nH and Q's up to 40 are shown to be feasible in very-large-scale-integration silicon technology" (IBM thick-copper wiring; a best case). [Established]
- **Yue and Wong**, *IEEE JSSC* 33(5):743–752, May 1998, DOI 10.1109/4.668989: "At 1-2 GHz, the addition of the shield increases the inductor quality factor up to 33% and reduces the substrate coupling between two adjacent inductors by as much as 25 dB ... the quality factor of a 2-GHz LC tank can be nearly doubled with a shielded inductor." [Established]
- **Niknejad and Meyer**, *IEEE JSSC* 33(10):1470–1481, 1998, DOI 10.1109/4.720393 (ASITIC; measurements 100 MHz to 5 GHz). [Established]
- **Mohan, Hershenson, Boyd and Lee**, *IEEE JSSC* 34(10):1419–1424, 1999, DOI 10.1109/4.792620: "Our simple expression matches the field solver inductance values typically within around 3%"; previous expressions have "typical errors around 20% (or more)". [Established]
- **Burghartz and Rejaei**, *IEEE TED* 50(3):718–729, Mar. 2003, DOI 10.1109/TED.2003.810474 (Q, self-resonance and area across technologies; substrate-resistivity regimes). [Established]
- A "typical" standard-CMOS inductor Q (often quoted as roughly 5–20 at 1–10 GHz) was **not** found in a primary source; the chapter uses 8 to 20 only as working assumptions.

## R-367 · Noise figure and two-port noise theory, measurement and zero-IF linearity: primary references (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: Crossref and OpenAlex metadata and abstracts; Agilent AN 57-1 (2000 edition) read from an archival copy (Keysight's current edition is gated).*

- **Friis**, "Noise Figures of Radio Receivers," *Proc. IRE* 32(7):419–422, Jul. 1944, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1944.232049: "A rigorous definition of the noise figure of radio receivers is given ... can be applied to four-terminal networks in general." [Established]
- **Rothe and Dahlke**, "Theory of Noisy Fourpoles," *Proc. IRE* 44(6):811–818, Jun. 1956, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1956.274998: a noise-free fourpole preceded by a noise fourpole with "the equivalent noise conductance G., the equivalent noise resistance Rn and the complex correlation admittance Ycor." [Established]
- **IRE Subcommittee 7.9 on Noise (Haus, chair)**, "Representation of Noise in Linear Twoports," *Proc. IRE* 48(1):69–74, Jan. 1960, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1960.287381: "the noise factor is then shown to be a function of four parameters which are independent of the circuit external to the twoport." Companion "IRE Standards on Methods of Measuring Noise in Linear Twoports, 1959," DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1960.287380. [Established]
- **Agilent AN 57-1**, "Fundamentals of RF and Microwave Noise Figure Measurements," 5952-8255E, Oct. 2000: 290 K reference; "The power spectral density kTo ... is the even number 4.00 x 10-21 watts per hertz of bandwidth (–174 dBm/Hz). The IRE ... adopted 290K"; "ENR is not the 'on' noise relative to kTB as is often erroneously believed"; "An ENR of 0 dB corresponds to Th = 580K"; F = ENR/(Y − 1) and F = [ENR − Y(Tc/T0 − 1)]/(Y − 1); "Generally the Y-factor method is not used when the noise figure is more than 10 dB above the ENR of the noise source"; "The absolute power level accuracy of the measuring device is not important since a ratio is to be measured." [Established]
- **Keysight PNA-X Pro** data sheet (Aug 2026, with R-362): built-in noise receiver; options add "a built-in noise tuner" for noise-parameter (source-pull) measurements. [Established]
- **Shaeffer and Lee**, *IEEE JSSC* 32(5):745–759, May 1997, DOI 10.1109/4.568846: "a forward gain (S21) of 22 dB with a noise figure of only 3.5 dB while drawing 30 mW from a 1.5 V supply" (0.6 µm CMOS, 1.5 GHz GPS). [Established]
- **Razavi**, *IEEE TCAS-II* 44(6):428–435, Jun. 1997, DOI 10.1109/82.592569 ("dc offset, I/Q mismatch, even-order distortion, flicker noise, and oscillator leakage"); **Abidi**, *IEEE JSSC* 30(12):1399–1410, Dec. 1995, DOI 10.1109/4.482187. [Established]

## R-368 · 3GPP NR handset receiver requirements and the sensitivity derivation (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: TS 38.101-1 V19.7.0 (2026-09) and TR 38.817-01 V16.4.0 (2022-09), DOCX from the 3GPP FTP archive.*

- **TS 38.101-1 V19.7.0** §7.3: REFSENS is "the minimum mean power applied to each one of the UE antenna ports ... at which the throughput shall meet or exceed the requirements for the specified reference measurement channel"; throughput "≥ 95 %"; QPSK. Table 7.3.2-1a (two antenna ports, FDD), n1 at 15 kHz SCS: −100.0 / −96.8 / −93.8 / −89.6 dBm for 5 / 10 / 20 / 50 MHz; NOTE 2 "The transmitter shall be set to PUMAX". Table 7.3.2-1b: n78 at 30 kHz "−96.1 + 10log10(NRB/24)" dBm. NOTE 8: "The REFSENS value is rounded to the nearest number down to one decimal point." 4Rx and 8Rx allowances (Tables 7.3.2-2, 7.3.2-2a) and the XR two-port allowance −1.0 dB (Table 7.3.2-2b). [Established]
- **Interference tests (same TS):** ACS (§7.5) 33 dB for 3–10 MHz below 2.7 GHz, interferers up to −25 dBm; in-band blocking (§7.6.2) −56 / −44 dBm; out-of-band CW blocking (§7.6.3) −44 / −30 / −15 dBm by range (−20 dBm above 6 GHz); narrow-band blocking (§7.6.4) −55 dBm CW; wide-band intermodulation (§7.8.2) "PInterferer 1 (CW)" −46 dBm and "PInterferer 2 (Modulated)" −46 dBm with FInterferer 2 = 2 × FInterferer 1, wanted at REFSENS + 6 to 9 dB; maximum input level (§7.4) −25 dBm. [Established]
- **TR 38.817-01 V16.4.0** §6.2: "Sensitivity = -174dBm(kT) + 10*log(RX BW) + NF + SNR +IM – diversity gain"; SNR "−1dB"; "the diversity gain is 3dB and the Implementation Margin (IM) uses 2.5dB"; "The NF for the NR bands can reuse those for E-UTRA. For the three new NR bands, i.e. Bands n77~79, the agreed NR values are 10.5dB, 10dB and 10dB respectively." The 9 dB for legacy bands is inferred by reconstructing the n1 values (−99.97 and −96.79 dBm before rounding); no direct statement was found (open issue). [Established for quoted text; Industry estimate for the inferred 9 dB]

## R-371 · LNA classics, the short-channel noise-factor debate and LNA figures of merit (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: Crossref and OpenAlex metadata and abstracts; Belostotski and Klumperink (2022) read in full from the University of Twente repository.*

- **Shaeffer and Lee**, *IEEE JSSC* 32(5):745–759, May 1997, DOI 10.1109/4.568846: "a forward gain (S21) of 22 dB with a noise figure of only 3.5 dB while drawing 30 mW from a 1.5 V supply" (0.6 µm CMOS, 1.5 GHz GPS), with "a discussion on the effects of induced gate noise". Corrections: *IEEE JSSC* 40(6):1397–1398, Jun. 2005, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2005.848626. [Established]
- **Bruccoleri, Klumperink and Nauta**, *IEEE JSSC* 39(2):275–282, Feb. 2004, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2003.821786: "a fundamental tradeoff between noise figure (NF) and source impedance matching, which limits the NF to values typically above 3 dB"; NF below 2.4 dB from 150 to 2000 MHz, 13.7 dB voltage gain, IIP3 0 dBm, IIP2 +12 dBm, 14 mA from 2.5 V (0.25 µm CMOS). [Established]
- **Belostotski and Haslett**, *IEEE JSSC* 42(11):2492–2502, Nov. 2007, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2007.907172: "sub-0.2 dB noise figure from 800 MHz to 1400 MHz" (90 nm CMOS), with "the required signal-source resistance ... presented by the telescope custom-made antenna elements". [Established]
- **Abidi**, *IEEE TED* 33(11):1801–1805, Nov. 1986, DOI 10.1109/T-ED.1986.22743: noise "greater by factors of 2 to 4 than the noise expected from long-channel noise theory". **Scholten et al.**, *IEEE TED* 50(3):618–632, Mar. 2003, DOI 10.1109/TED.2003.810480: "we find only a moderate enhancement of the drain current noise for short-channel MOSFETs. The gate current noise on the other hand is more significantly enhanced, which is explained by the effects of the gate resistance." Also Knoblinger et al. (JSSC 2001, DOI 10.1109/4.918922), Chen and Deen (TED 2002, DOI 10.1109/TED.2002.801229), Navid et al. (J. Appl. Phys. 2007, DOI 10.1063/1.2740345). No recent FinFET/FDSOI value found. [Contested]
- **Belostotski and Klumperink**, *IEEE TCAS-II* 69(3):734–738, Mar. 2022, DOI 10.1109/TCSII.2021.3113607: "over 30 different FoMs were identified"; dataset of "nearly 500" (abstract) or "more than 500" (introduction) CMOS LNAs over 20 years; eq. (1) Fmin = 1 + 2(ω0/ωT)√(δγ(1−|c|²)/5), credited to Lee's textbook, with long-channel γ = 2/3, δ = 4/3, c = j0.395. [Established]

J.* 39(5):1321–1350, Sep. 1960, DOI 10.1002/j.1538-7305.1960.tb03962.x (N-path filter). **Ghaffari, Klumperink, Soer and Nauta**, *IEEE JSSC* 46(5):998–1010, May 2011, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2011.2117010: "The switching frequency defines the center frequency, while the RC-time and duty cycle of the clock define the bandwidth"; 4-path, 65 nm, tunable 0.1–1 GHz, IIP3 > 14 dBm, NF 3–5 dB, 2–16 mW clock power. - **Andrews and Molnar**, *IEEE JSSC* 45(12):2696–2708, Dec. 2010, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2010.2077151: "70 dB of gain, NF as low as 3 dB, and 25 dBm out-of-band IIP3", 0.1–1.3 GHz. **van Zanten, van der Zee and Nauta**, ISSCC 2024, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49657.2024.10454419: ">20dBm adjacent-channel IIP3 consuming less than 25mW". **Gadelkarim and Mercier**, ISSCC 2025, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC49661.2025.10904619: mm-wave mixer-first N-path receivers cost "13 to 89mW and 8 to 20dB"; "there are no SAW-equivalent filters available at these frequencies". [Established]

## R-377 · Oscillator theory classics: Leeson, Razavi, Hajimiri–Lee, Kinget's FoM, Adler, Groszkowski (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026: Crossref metadata and OpenAlex abstracts (by a research subagent, re-checked by the writer for Adler, Razavi 2004 and Groszkowski); Kinget's chapter preprint from the author's site.*

- **Leeson**, "A simple model of feedback oscillator noise spectrum," *Proc. IEEE* 54(2):329–330, Feb. 1966, DOI 10.1109/PROC.1966.4682 (the text itself was not read; the chapter derives the formula rather than quoting it); **Leeson**, "Oscillator phase noise: A 50-year review," *IEEE Trans. UFFC* 63(8):1208–1225, Aug. 2016, DOI 10.1109/TUFFC.2016.2562663. [Established]
- **Razavi**, "A study of phase noise in CMOS oscillators," *IEEE JSSC* 31(3):331–343, Mar. 1996, DOI 10.1109/4.494195. [Established]
- **Hajimiri and Lee**, "A general theory of phase noise in electrical oscillators," *IEEE JSSC* 33(2):179–194, Feb. 1998, DOI 10.1109/4.658619 (corrections DOI 10.1109/4.678662): works "by acknowledging the true periodically time-varying nature of all oscillators" and "explains the details of how 1/f noise in a device upconverts into close-in phase noise". **Lee and Hajimiri**, "Oscillator phase noise: A tutorial," *IEEE JSSC* 35(3):326–336, Mar. 2000, DOI 10.1109/4.826814: "identifies the importance of symmetry in suppressing the upconversion of 1/f noise". [Established]
- **Kinget**, "Integrated GHz voltage controlled oscillators," in *Analog Circuit Design*, Kluwer, 1999, pp. 353–381, DOI 10.1007/978-1-4757-3047-0_17: FM = 10 log[(f0/Δf)²/(L·P)] with P in mW; "The power consumption P is for the oscillator core only and does not include the output buffer". Described here as "popularized by", not invented by. [Established]
- **Adler**, "A study of locking phenomena in oscillators," *Proc. IRE* 34(6):351–357, Jun. 1946, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1946.229930; **Razavi**, "A study of injection locking and pulling in oscillators," *IEEE JSSC* 39(9):1415–1424, Sep. 2004, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2004.831608 (citations only; the chapter derives the lock range and Adler's equation itself). [Established]
- **Groszkowski**, "The interdependence of frequency variation and harmonic content, and the problem of constant-frequency oscillators," *Proc. IRE* 21(7):958–981, Jul. 1933, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1933.227821 (citation only; the chapter derives the shift). [Established]

## R-378 · LC oscillator topology milestones: tail filtering, Colpitts vs LC tank, class-C, class-F (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026: Crossref metadata and OpenAlex abstracts.*

- **Hegazi, Sjöland and Abidi**, *IEEE JSSC* 36(12):1921–1930, Dec. 2001, DOI 10.1109/4.972142: a passive LC filter lowers "the phase-noise factor in a differential oscillator to its fundamental minimum"; two 1.1 GHz VCOs at −153 dBc/Hz at 3 MHz, 3.7 mA from 2.5 V, which "exceed discrete transistor modules in figure of merit" (FoM 194.6 dB, our arithmetic). [Established]
- **Andreani, Wang, Vandi and Fard**, *IEEE JSSC* 40(5):1107–1118, May 2005, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2005.845991: theory gives the LC-tank oscillator "a 2-dB lower phase-noise figure-of-merit" than the differential Colpitts under simplified assumptions; best LC tank −142 dBc/Hz at 3 MHz from 2.9 GHz at 16 mW, "FoM of ∼189 dBc/Hz"; the differential Colpitts' measured FoM "is ∼5 dB lower". [Established]
- **Mazzanti and Andreani**, *IEEE JSSC* 43(12):2716–2729, Dec. 2008, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2008.2004867: "a theoretical 3.9 dB phase noise improvement compared to the standard differential-pair LC-tank oscillator is achieved for the same current consumption". **Fanori and Andreani**, *IEEE JSSC* 48(7):1730–1740, Jul. 2013, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2013.2253402: 90 nm, 3.4–4.5 GHz, 5.5 mA from 1.2 V, below −152 dBc/Hz at 20 MHz from 4 GHz, "The resulting FoM is 191 dBc/Hz" (189.8 dB recomputed with exactly −152 dBc/Hz). [Established]
- **Babaie and Staszewski**, *IEEE JSSC* 48(12):3120–3133, Dec. 2013, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2013.2273823: "enforcing a pseudo-square voltage waveform around the LC tank by increasing the third-harmonic"; "the effective impulse sensitivity function (ISF) decreases"; 65 nm, −136 dBc/Hz average at 3 MHz over 5.9–7.6 GHz, FoM 192 dBc/Hz, 12 mA from 1.25 V, 0.12 mm². Class-F2: *IEEE JSSC* 50(3):679–692, Mar. 2015, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2014.2379265: −139 dBc/Hz at 3 MHz from 8.7 GHz, 191 dBc/Hz FoM. [Established]

## R-382 · Power-amplifier classics: Doherty, Chireix, Kahn, Sokal, Raab, Cripps, DPD models (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: Crossref metadata and OpenAlex abstracts; Cripps' book from a library record.*

- **Doherty**, "A new high efficiency power amplifier for modulated waves," *Proc. IRE* 24(9):1163–1182, Sep. 1936, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1936.228468: "Plate circuit efficiencies of sixty to sixty-five per cent independent of modulation are obtained by means of the combined action of varying load distribution among the tubes and varying circuit impedance over the modulation cycle"; a 50 kW transmitter. Reviews: **Pengelly, Fager and Ozen**, *IEEE Microwave Mag.* 17(2):41–58, Feb. 2016, DOI 10.1109/MMM.2015.2498081; **Camarchia et al.**, *IEEE T-MTT* 63(2):559–571, Feb. 2015, DOI 10.1109/TMTT.2014.2387061. [Established]
- **Chireix**, "High power outphasing modulation," *Proc. IRE* 23(11):1370–1392, Nov. 1935, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1935.227299. **Kahn**, "Single-sideband transmission by envelope elimination and restoration," *Proc. IRE* 40(7):803–806, Jul. 1952, DOI 10.1109/JRPROC.1952.273844: the phase component is "amplified by means of Class-C amplifiers, and the amplitude envelope is restored at the final amplifier". [Established]
- **Sokal and Sokal**, "Class E—A new class of high-efficiency tuned single-ended switching power amplifiers," *IEEE JSSC* 10(3):168–176, Jun. 1975, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.1975.1050582: "96 percent transistor efficiency at 3.9 MHz at 26-W output". [Established]
- **Raab**, "Class-F power amplifiers with maximally flat waveforms," *IEEE T-MTT* 45(11):2007–2012, Nov. 1997, DOI 10.1109/22.644215; "Class-E, class-C, and class-F power amplifiers based upon a finite number of harmonics," *IEEE T-MTT* 49(8):1462–1468, Aug. 2001, DOI 10.1109/22.939927: "the maximum achievable efficiency depends not upon the class of operation, but upon the number of harmonics employed" (a published comment exists: DOI 10.1109/TMTT.2009.2019999). **Raab et al.**, "Power amplifiers and transmitters for RF and microwave," *IEEE T-MTT* 50(3):814–826, Mar. 2002, DOI 10.1109/22.989965. [Established]
- **Cripps**, *RF Power Amplifiers for Wireless Communications*, 2nd ed., Artech House, 2006 (library record; the publisher page returned 403). [Established]
- **DPD models:** **Ding et al.**, *IEEE Trans. Commun.* 52(1):159–165, Jan. 2004, DOI 10.1109/TCOMM.2003.822188 (memory polynomial with indirect learning); **Morgan et al.**, *IEEE Trans. Signal Process.* 54(10):3852–3860, Oct. 2006, DOI 10.1109/TSP.2006.879264 (generalized memory polynomial): amplifiers "must be backed off considerably from their peak power level in order to control out-of-band spurious emissions". [Established]

Mag.* 33(5):26–38, May 1995, DOI 10.1109/35.393001: "the canonical functional partitioning of channel coding into antenna, RF, IF, baseband, and bitstream segments". **Abidi**, *IEEE JSSC* 42(5):954–966, May 2007, DOI 10.1109/JSSC.2007.894307: "it is enough to receive one channel with any bandwidth, situated in any band"; a 90 nm prototype tuning 800 MHz–6 GHz. **Mirabbasi and Martin**, *IEEE Commun. Mag.* 38(11):132–139, Nov. 2000, DOI 10.1109/35.883502. **Bhagavatula**, *IEEE SSC Mag.* 13(1):35–47, 2021, DOI 10.1109/MSSC.2020.3036144. [Established]
- **Walden**, *IEEE JSAC* 17(4):539–550, Apr. 1999, DOI 10.1109/49.761034: resolution "falls off by ~1 bit for every doubling of the sampling rate", attributed to "uncertainty in the sampling instant due to aperture jitter". [Established]

Ruppel, "Acoustic Wave Filter Technology–A Review," *IEEE TUFFC* 64(9):1390–1400, Sep 2017, DOI 10.1109/TUFFC.2017.2690905** [Established]: "In the frequency range up to 1 GHz, surface acoustic wave (SAW) filters are almost exclusively employed, while in the higher frequency range, bulk acoustic wave (BAW) and SAW filters are competing for their shares"; smartphones need filter functions "ranging from the low double-digit range up to almost triple digit numbers" [Industry estimate, 2017].
- **R. Ruby, P. Bradley, J. D. Larson, Y. Oshmyansky, "PCS 1900 MHz duplexer using thin film bulk acoustic resonators (FBARs)," *Electronics Letters* 35(10):794–795, May 1999, DOI 10.1049/el:19990559** [Established]: "An ultra-miniature duplexer (5 × 8 × 2.2 mm³)"; "Isolation between the Tx and Rx ports exceeds 50 dB over most of the Tx band and 46 dB in the Rx band. The FBAR filters handle up to 2 W throughout the passband."
- **J. D. Larson et al., "Power handling and temperature coefficient studies in FBAR duplexers for the 1900 MHz PCS band," *IEEE Ultrasonics Symp.* 2000, pp. 869–874, DOI 10.1109/ULTSYM.2000.922680** [Established]: Tx ladder loss "~3 dB (343 mW dissipated maximum)", "~1 MWatts/cm³" per FBAR; "The measured resonator frequency temperature coefficient is ~27 ppm/°C" (sign not shown in the abstract); withstands "+36 dBm (4 Watts)".
- **T. Yokoyama et al. (Fujitsu), "New electrode material for low-loss and high-Q FBAR filters," *IEEE Ultrasonics Symp.* 2004, pp. 429–432, DOI 10.1109/ULTSYM.2004.1417754** [Established]: "an unloaded resonant quality factor, Q_r, of 3585 and an effective electromechanical coupling coefficient, k², of 6.6% at 5 GHz".
- **T. Takai et al. (Murata), "High-Performance SAW Resonator on New Multilayered Substrate Using LiTaO₃ Crystal," *IEEE TUFFC* 64(9):1382–1389, Sep 2017, DOI 10.1109/TUFFC.2017.2738119** [Established]: "a Bode-Q over 4000 and TCF of -8 ppm/°C, which are four times higher than and one-fifth as small as those of a conventional 4° YX-LT SAW resonator"; "a band 25 duplexer with very narrow duplex gap was successfully developed". The chapter's −40 ppm/°C for plain LiTaO₃ SAW is implied by "one-fifth" (author's arithmetic).
- **K. Hashimoto et al., "Recent development of temperature compensated SAW devices," *IEEE IUS* 2011, pp. 79–86, DOI 10.1109/ULTSYM.2011.0021** [Established]: SiO₂ deposition and wafer bonding as the compensation techniques.
- **R. Aigner, "SAW and BAW technologies for RF filter applications: A review of the relative strengths and weaknesses," *IEEE Ultrasonics Symp.* 2008, pp. 582–589, DOI 10.1109/ULTSYM.2008.0140** (further study).

## R-401 · Millimetre-wave propagation models and measurements (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: ITU PDFs, the 3GPP DOCX and a course-hosted copy of Rappaport 2013.*

- **Rec. ITU-R P.676-13 (08/2022)** [Established]: "Near 60 GHz, many oxygen absorption lines merge together at sea-level pressures to form a single, broad absorption band". The Recommendation gives curves, not a value table; the subagent evaluated its Annex 1 line-by-line model (44 oxygen and 35 water-vapour lines, standard atmosphere 1013.25 hPa, 15 °C, 7.5 g/m³): 0.101 dB/km at 28 GHz, 0.122 at 39 GHz, 14.66 at 60 GHz (peak 15.06 at 60.8 GHz), 0.388 at 73 GHz. The chapter quotes these only rounded (about 0.1 and about 15 dB/km); an independent implementation (e.g., the `itur` package) was not available to cross-check (open issue).
- **Rec. ITU-R P.838-3 (03/2005)** [Established]: $\gamma_R = kR^\alpha$; Table 5 coefficients (kH, αH): 28 GHz 0.2051, 0.9679; 39 GHz 0.4215, 0.8743; 60 GHz 0.8606, 0.7656. At 25 mm/h these give 4.62, 7.03 and 10.12 dB/km (verified in verify_21_10.py).
- **3GPP TR 38.901 V19.5.0 (2026-09)** [Established model]: UMi street canyon LOS "PL1=32.4+21log10(d3D)+20log10(fc)"; NLOS "35.3log10(d3D)+22.4 + 21.3log10(fc)-0.3(hUT-1.5)" with σ_SF 7.82 dB; self-blocking "The attenuation of each cluster due to self-blocking … is 30 dB"; penetration "Lglass=2+0.2f", "Lconcrete=5+4f".
- **T. S. Rappaport et al., "Millimeter Wave Mobile Communications for 5G Cellular: It Will Work!", *IEEE Access* 1:335–349, 2013, DOI 10.1109/ACCESS.2013.2260813** [Established, one campaign]: LOS PLE 1.68 (best) and 2.55; NLOS 4.58 (best) and 5.76; "57% of locations were outages … with most outages occurring beyond 200 m from the TX". Its 28 GHz rain figure (7 dB/km at 25 mm/h) differs from P.838-3 (4.6 dB/km); it gives clear glass as both 3.6 and 3.9 dB.
- **Sources:** [P.676-13](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/p/R-REC-P.676-13-202208-I!!PDF-E.pdf); [P.838-3](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/p/R-REC-P.838-3-200503-I!!PDF-E.pdf); [TR 38.901](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.901/38901-j50.zip); DOI above. ## R-406 · Bluetooth LE requirements and receiver data sheets (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent: the Bluetooth Core Specification HTML, the SIG range page and vendor data sheets (some via distributor copies).*

- **Bluetooth Core Specification v6.2 (2025-11-03), Vol 6 Part A** [Established]: "The reference sensitivity level referred to in this section is -70 dBm"; Table 4.1 BER by maximum payload (1–37 bytes: 0.1 %; 38–63: 0.064 %; 64–127: 0.034 %; 128–255: 0.017 %); Table 4.2: uncoded ≤ −70, Coded S=2 ≤ −75, Coded S=8 ≤ −82 dBm; transmit power "between 0.01 mW (-20 dBm) and 100 mW (+20 dBm)"; power classes 1, 1.5, 2, 3. ## R-415 · NTN standards and satellite-to-phone services (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent.*

- **3GPP "NTN overview" page** [Established]: "Release 17 (ASN.1 frozen in June 2022) was the first release with normative requirements for NTN"; "Only transparent payload"; bands "n255 (L-band) and n256 (S-band)"; "Handheld devices with power class 3 are supported"; "UEs with GNSS capabilities are assumed"; "the UE shall autonomously pre-compensate the Timing Advance, as well as the frequency Doppler shift"; HARQ processes "can be increased to 32 (e.g., in NGSO satellite systems)"; Rel-19 adds regenerative payloads.
- **Apple Newsroom, 15 Nov 2022** [Apple statement]: "users can send and receive messages in as little as 15 seconds in clear conditions"; "it can take a few minutes for even short messages to get through".

## R-417 · UWB and Bluetooth ranging (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent.*

- **IEEE SA page for IEEE 802.15.4z-2020** [Established]: "Enhanced Ultra Wideband (UWB) Physical Layers (PHYs) and Associated Ranging Techniques"; board approval 2020-06-04, published 2020-08-25; superseded, with P802.15.4ab in progress (sensing, "at least 50 Mbit/s").
- **D. Coppens et al., *IEEE Access* 10:70219–70241, 2022, DOI 10.1109/ACCESS.2022.3187410** [Established]: HRP channel 5 at 6489.6 MHz and channel 9 at 7987.2 MHz, each 499.2 MHz wide; the STS field supports secure ranging.
- **Apple Newsroom, 12 Sep 2023** [Apple claim]: the second-generation UWB chip connects "at three times the range as before". **CCC press release, 26 Jul 2022** [Established]: Digital Key Release 3 provides "hands-free, location-aware keyless access … through the implementation of UWB in combination with Bluetooth Low Energy".
- **TI application note SWRA791 (Feb 2024)** [Established]: Bluetooth Channel Sounding Phase-Based Ranging; "72 channels with 1MHz channel spacing can be used". Bluetooth Core 6.1/6.2 feature details (randomized RPA updates; minimum connection interval "from 7.5 ms to 375 µs") were seen only in secondary sources (CNX Software, Help Net Security) because bluetooth.com refused automated access [Established, secondary].
- **Sources:** [IEEE 802.15.4z](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/802.15.4z/10230/); [Coppens et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.02190); [Apple 2023](https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2023/09/apple-debuts-iphone-15-and-iphone-15-plus/); [CCC](https://carconnectivity.org/press-release/car-connectivity-consortium-delivers-digital-key-release-3-0-specification/); [TI SWRA791](https://www.ti.com/lit/pdf/swra791). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-418 · GNSS signal structure and dual-frequency phones (Course 21)

*Read on 24 Nov 2026 by a research subagent.*

- **IS-GPS-705J (1 Aug 2022)** [Established]: L5 codes "with a chipping rate of 10.23 Mbps … the 10,230 chip code"; carrier "1176.45 MHz, or 115 times 10.23 MHz"; minimum received power "-157.0 dBW" (GPS III/IIIF). L1 C/A (1.023 Mcps, 1575.42 MHz) is from IS-GPS-200N (R-411). L2's 1227.60 MHz (120 × 10.23 MHz) is GPS's long-standing L2 carrier, also defined in IS-GPS-200N; the clause stating it was not re-read in this session [Established, not re-read].
- **Galileo OS SIS ICD Issue 2.2 (Nov 2025)** [Established]: carriers "E1 1575.420 … E5a 1176.450" MHz; E5a chip rate "10.230" Mchip/s; E1-B/C "1.023" Mcps; a new "E5a-QP" component.
- **EUSPA news, 4 Jun 2018** [Established that the device shipped; accuracy is a claim]: "the Xiaomi Mi 8, launched on May 31, is the world's first smartphone providing up to decimetre-level accuracy" with a "dual-frequency (E1/L1+E5/L5) GNSS chip".
- **Sources:** [IS-GPS-705J](https://www.gps.gov/sites/default/files/2025-07/IS-GPS-705J.pdf); [Galileo ICD 2.2](https://www.gsc-europa.eu/sites/default/files/sites/all/files/Galileo_OS_SIS_ICD_v2.2.pdf); [EUSPA](https://www.euspa.europa.eu/newsroom-events/news/worlds-first-dual-frequency-gnss-smartphone-hits-market). ## R-419 · NXP's exit from RF power (Course 21 audit)

*Search results only, 24 Nov 2026 (main agent and an audit subagent); the articles and NXP's own release were not opened. Medium confidence.*

- **Bits & Chips, 11 Dec 2025; also KTAR, ABC15, EverythingRF and Altium's supplier map** (search snippets): NXP is winding down its radio (RF) power business and closing its 150 mm RF-GaN fab in Chandler, Arizona (opened 2020), with final GaN wafers around Q1 2027 and a transition period for customers; the rest of the Chandler site continues. ## R-420 · GNSS carrier frequencies beyond GPS and Galileo (Course 21 audit)

*Search results, 24 Nov 2026 (main agent); the BeiDou and GLONASS ICDs themselves were not opened. QZSS was not confirmed and is not claimed.*

- **ESA Navipedia, "BeiDou Signal Plan"** [Established; secondary source]: B1I carrier 1561.098 MHz (BPSK); B1C centred at 1575.42 MHz; B2a centred at 1176.45 MHz. The page cites the separate BeiDou ICDs for each signal.
- **ESA Navipedia, "GLONASS Signal Plan," and ITU-R M.1787** [Established]: legacy GLONASS L1 FDMA carriers at 1602 + k × 0.5625 MHz, so they do not coincide with the GPS and Galileo L1 band.
- **Sources:** [Navipedia BeiDou](https://gssc.esa.int/navipedia/index.php/BeiDou_Signal_Plan); [Navipedia GLONASS](https://gssc.esa.int/navipedia/index.php/GLONASS_Signal_Plan); [ITU-R M.1787-0](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/m/R-REC-M.1787-0-200908-S!!PDF-E.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.
